SECTION 1: PROJECT OVERVIEW

1.1 Project Name
CA-OS — The AI Operating System for Indian Chartered Accountant Firms
Tagline: "Run your entire CA practice from one intelligent platform."

1.2 Vision
To become the definitive practice management intelligence layer for every Chartered Accountant firm in India — eliminating the chaos of Excel sheets, WhatsApp groups, and scattered tools, and replacing them with a single AI-powered platform that automates document collection, compliance tracking, staff coordination, client communication, and billing.
Within five years, CA-OS will be the operating system of record for CA practice management across India and the Global South, processing over ₹10,000 crore in client billings annually and serving over 50,000 CA firms.

1.3 Mission
To give every Indian CA — from a solo practitioner in Warangal to a 50-partner firm in Mumbai — the operational leverage of a Big Four firm, powered by AI agents that never sleep, never miss a deadline, and never forget to follow up.

1.4 Business Problem
India has 3.9 lakh registered Chartered Accountants managing an estimated 2–3 crore businesses for compliance, GST, TDS, ITR, audit, and advisory services. The operational reality of 95% of CA firms is:
Problem 1 — Deadline Management Chaos: GST returns, TDS filings, ITR deadlines, audit schedules, and FEMA filings create a web of 200+ annual deadlines per client. CAs managing 50–200 clients have thousands of deadlines to track. Current solution: a mix of WhatsApp reminders, personal memory, and Excel calendars that fail constantly.
Problem 2 — Document Collection Paralysis: Every monthly GST filing requires bank statements, purchase registers, sales invoices, and e-way bills from clients. CAs spend 20–40% of their working hours manually chasing these documents via WhatsApp. Clients are unresponsive. Documents arrive at the last minute. Filings are rushed.
Problem 3 — Staff Coordination Blindness: CA firms rely on articleship students (paid ₹5,000–15,000/month) to execute most compliance work. Assigning, tracking, and reviewing this work happens through verbal instructions and WhatsApp messages. There is zero visibility into who is doing what, what is overdue, and where work is stacking up.
Problem 4 — Client Communication Inefficiency: Responding to client queries (GST notices, ITR questions, advance tax calculations) requires the CA to recall context, draft responses manually, and send them via WhatsApp or email. This consumes hours that should go to advisory work.
Problem 5 — Billing Leakage: CA firms consistently under-bill because they have no systematic record of actual time and work done per client. Advisory calls, notice responses, special certificates — these often go unbilled. Research suggests Indian CA firms lose 20–35% of billable revenue due to poor billing hygiene.
Problem 6 — No AI Leverage: While global tools like Karbon and TaxDome exist, they are built for UK/US markets, priced in dollars, and have no native understanding of Indian GST architecture, Indian compliance deadlines, or the WhatsApp-first communication culture of Indian clients.
The total cost of this problem: A mid-sized CA firm managing 100 clients loses approximately ₹8–15 lakh annually in billing leakage, 2–4 additional staff who could be replaced by automation, and a significant number of clients who leave due to poor responsiveness and missed deadlines.

1.5 Target Users
Primary Users:

Practicing Chartered Accountants (CA) with 20–500 active clients
CA firm partners (sole proprietor to 10-partner firms)
Articleship students working in CA firms
Qualified CA staff members working in practice

Secondary Users:

Business clients of CA firms (SME owners, directors, promoters)
Company Secretaries (CS) — Phase 2 expansion
Cost and Management Accountants (CMA) — Phase 3 expansion

Excluded from Scope (v1.0):

Individual taxpayers filing their own returns
Big Four or large network firms (they have custom ERP systems)
Academicians and CA coaching institutes


1.6 Industry

Financial Technology (FinTech)
Legal Technology (LegalTech)
Professional Services Automation (PSA)
Compliance Technology (ComplianceTech)
B2B SaaS

Primary regulatory context: India — Income Tax Act 1961, GST Act 2017, Companies Act 2013, FEMA 1999, and all associated rules and notifications.

1.7 Real-World Use Cases
Use Case 1 — Monthly GST Filing Cycle:
On the 1st of every month, CA-OS automatically identifies all clients due for GSTR-1 filing on the 11th and GSTR-3B on the 20th. It sends personalized WhatsApp messages to each client requesting their sales register, purchase register, bank statement, and e-way bill summary. As documents arrive, the system logs them, extracts key figures using OCR, and marks tasks complete. Staff are auto-assigned to prepare each return. On the 8th, the system re-sends reminders to clients whose documents are still pending. The CA reviews a consolidated compliance health dashboard showing all 87 clients with their document readiness, preparation status, and filing status — without opening a single WhatsApp chat manually.
Use Case 2 — GST Notice Response:
A client forwards a GST demand notice to their CA on WhatsApp at 10pm. The CA is offline. CA-OS receives the document (via WhatsApp webhook), classifies it as a GST demand notice, extracts the notice number, demand amount, and due date for reply. It auto-creates a task for the CA, sends an acknowledgment to the client saying "Your notice has been received and logged. Your CA will respond within 2 working days," and drafts a preliminary response using the AI draft generator pre-loaded with the client's GST history and the notice details. The next morning, the CA reviews the AI-drafted response, makes minor edits, and sends it to the client in one click.
Use Case 3 — Annual Audit Planning:
In August, CA-OS automatically generates an audit preparation checklist for all corporate clients whose financial year closes in March. It sends a document request schedule to each client over 4 weeks — week 1 requesting trial balance and bank statements, week 2 requesting fixed asset register and loans schedule, and so on. Staff are assigned to each phase. The CA tracks overall audit readiness on a single dashboard.
Use Case 4 — Billing Recovery:
At month end, the CA-OS billing module compiles an auto-generated invoice for each client based on: fixed retainer fee + time logged by staff on client tasks + ad-hoc work items added during the month. The CA reviews, adjusts if needed, and sends the invoice via Razorpay payment link directly from the platform. Payment status updates automatically. Overdue invoices trigger automated follow-up messages.
Use Case 5 — New Client Onboarding:
A new manufacturing client is added to CA-OS. The CA enters basic details: company name, GSTIN, PAN, registered address, constitution (Pvt Ltd), and compliance requirements (GST monthly + TDS quarterly + ITR annual + audit). CA-OS automatically generates the complete compliance calendar for the next 12 months for this client, creates all required compliance task templates, and sends the client a welcome message with a link to their client portal where they can upload their previous year's documents.

1.8 Monetization Strategy
Primary Revenue — SaaS Subscription:

Secondary Revenue Streams:

AI Draft Packs: Starter and Growth plans include limited AI drafts (50/month and 200/month). Additional packs at ₹999 for 500 drafts.
Setup & Onboarding Service: White-glove onboarding service at ₹5,000–15,000 one-time for firms that want assisted migration from their existing tools.
CA Association / Chapter Licenses: Bulk licensing to ICAI chapter bodies, CA associations, and network firms at negotiated annual rates.
Marketplace (Phase 2): A curated marketplace where CA firms can offer specialized services (FEMA advisory, international tax, valuation) to each other's clients, with CA-OS taking a 5% platform fee.
Data Intelligence Reports (Phase 3): Anonymized, aggregated industry benchmarks sold to CA associations, banking institutions, and policy bodies.

1.9 Competitive Advantages
Advantage 1 — India-Native Compliance Intelligence: The GST calendar engine is built from first principles on Indian compliance law. It knows the difference between GSTR-1 and GSTR-1A, the 30-day IRN rule, e-invoicing thresholds by AATO, TDS section codes, advance tax installment schedules, and due date shifting rules when deadlines fall on holidays. No global tool has this.
Advantage 2 — WhatsApp as the Operating Layer: Indian clients do not use email consistently. WhatsApp is where the real communication happens. CA-OS uses WhatsApp not just for notifications but as a two-way document collection and communication channel. No existing practice management tool has this deeply integrated.
Advantage 3 — AI Trained on Indian CA Context: The AI drafting and query response system is built with Indian CA context — it knows how to write a GST notice response in formal Indian English, how to structure a tax computation letter, how to phrase a penalty waiver request under GSTP-20. This is not a generic LLM wrapper.
Advantage 4 — Pricing for Indian Market: Priced at 20–30% of global competitor pricing, specifically calibrated to the Indian CA firm's revenue model.
Advantage 5 — WhatsApp-Mediated Client Relationships: By building the client communication layer into the platform, CA-OS creates a structural advantage — the CA's client relationships are mediated through the platform, creating deep switching costs.
Advantage 6 — Staff Workload Intelligence: The articleship student and staff workload tracking module is unique to CA-OS. No other tool tracks and optimizes how junior CA firm staff allocate their time across clients.

1.10 Scalability Vision
Year 1: Hyderabad + Telangana → 500 CA firms → ₹2–3 crore ARR
Year 2: Pan-India → 3,000 CA firms → ₹15–18 crore ARR. Launch CS (Company Secretary) module.
Year 3: 10,000 CA firms + CS firms → ₹50 crore ARR. Launch international expansion to UAE (Indian CA diaspora managing UAE VAT).
Year 4: Southeast Asia (Bangladesh, Sri Lanka, Malaysia — similar regulatory environments). White-label offering for CA associations and network firms. ₹120 crore ARR.
Year 5: Global South professional services platform. Potential acquisition by Zoho, Tally, or Intuit. ₹300+ crore ARR or strategic exit.

1.11 AI Usage in the Project
CA-OS is an AI-native product. AI is not a feature — it is the product's central value proposition. Specific AI capabilities:
AI Capability 1 — Deadline Sentinel Agent: Continuously monitors all client compliance calendars, identifies upcoming deadlines, and autonomously sends personalized reminders via WhatsApp to clients and alerts to CA staff. Operates 24/7 without human intervention.
AI Capability 2 — Document Chaser Agent: Sends personalized document request messages to clients on a configurable schedule. Tracks responses. Escalates unpredictable clients. Knows which document to request based on compliance type.
AI Capability 3 — Communication Draft Generator: Given a client query or incoming message, generates a professional, contextually appropriate response in seconds. Trained on Indian CA communication patterns and compliance language.
AI Capability 4 — Document Intelligence: Receives uploaded PDFs (bank statements, invoices, GST returns) and extracts structured data — totals, GST amounts, party names, dates. Flags anomalies and reconciliation mismatches.
AI Capability 5 — Compliance Notice Classifier: When a client uploads or forwards a government notice, automatically classifies the notice type (GST demand, scrutiny notice, income tax notice, FEMA query), extracts key details, and creates a prioritized action task.
AI Capability 6 — Billing Intelligence: Reviews unbilled work logs and suggests invoicing items the CA may have missed. Estimates fair billing amounts for time-tracked tasks based on historical patterns.
AI Capability 7 — Churn Prediction Agent: Monitors client engagement signals — response rates to document requests, payment delays, and query volumes — and predicts which clients are at risk of leaving. Alerts the CA proactively.
AI Capability 8 — Penalty Risk Scoring: Analyzes each client's document submission rate, historical compliance pattern, and pending tasks to generate a 1–10 risk score for penalty exposure. Updated monthly.

1.12 Future Roadmap
Phase 1 (Months 1–6) — MVP:
Core compliance calendar, WhatsApp document collection bot, task assignment board, client portal, basic billing module.
Phase 2 (Months 7–12) — AI Layer:
Full AI agent suite, document OCR and extraction, AI draft generator, penalty risk scoring, advanced analytics.
Phase 3 (Year 2) — Platform Expansion:
Company Secretary module, CMA module, multi-branch firm support, GST portal direct API sync, advanced audit module.
Phase 4 (Year 2–3) — Ecosystem:
CA-to-CA referral marketplace, client financial intelligence (MIS reports, cash flow analysis), white-label platform for associations.
Phase 5 (Year 3–5) — International:
UAE VAT module, Bangladesh NBR compliance, SaaS API (allow third-party integrations with Tally, Zoho Books, QuickBooks India).

SECTION 2: USER TYPES & ROLES

2.1 Role Architecture Overview
CA-OS implements a hybrid Role-Based Access Control (RBAC) + Attribute-Based Access Control (ABAC) model. Roles define baseline permissions. Attributes (firm membership, client assignment, subscription plan) further restrict or expand access within roles.
All roles exist within the context of a Firm (the tenant unit). The Super Admin role exists outside the firm context and is a platform-level role only.

2.2 Role Definitions
Role 1: Super Admin (Platform Level)
Who They Are: CA-OS internal operations team. Maximum 5 people. Has god-mode access to the entire platform across all tenants.
Permissions:

View, create, update, and delete any firm, user, client, or data record across all tenants
Access all audit logs across all firms
Manage platform-level configuration (feature flags, AI model settings, WhatsApp API credentials)
Access billing and subscription records for all firms
Create and manage CA-OS admin accounts
Access and export all analytics data
Trigger system-wide maintenance modes
Override any firm-level setting
Access all AI agent logs and conversation histories
Manage third-party API keys and integrations at platform level

Restrictions:

All Super Admin actions are logged with immutable audit trails
Accessing specific firm data requires a documented reason logged in the system
Cannot delete audit logs under any circumstance
Requires MFA on every login session
Sessions expire after 2 hours of inactivity
Actions must be performed from approved IP addresses only

Dashboard: Platform Operations Dashboard showing firm-level health, subscription metrics, system performance, AI usage across all tenants, error rates, billing revenue, and support ticket status.
Accessible Modules: All modules across all firms. Plus platform-exclusive modules: Firm Management, Subscription Management, Platform Analytics, System Configuration, AI Model Management, Audit Log Viewer.
Security Access: Highest clearance. Requires MFA + IP whitelist + hardware security key for destructive operations.
Data Visibility: All data across all firms. Personal data access is logged and requires justification.

Role 2: Firm Owner / Partner (Firm Level)
Who They Are: The practicing CA or managing partner who owns or administers the CA firm account on CA-OS. This is the primary account holder and billing decision maker.
Permissions:

Full administrative control over the firm's CA-OS account
Add, edit, deactivate, and reactivate all team members (staff and articleship)
Add, edit, archive, and delete client records
View and manage all compliance tasks across all clients
Access all financial data — billing logs, invoices, payment history
Configure WhatsApp bot settings, message templates, and automation rules
Access and export all AI drafts, conversation logs, and document histories
Manage firm settings: branding, subscription, API integrations
View all staff performance metrics and workload data
Create and manage custom compliance task templates
Manage client portal settings and access
Access all analytics and reporting
Configure notification preferences for the entire firm

Restrictions:

Cannot access CA-OS platform-level settings
Cannot view other firms' data
Cannot modify Super Admin accounts
Subscription changes require Razorpay payment confirmation

Dashboard: Firm Command Center — Morning brief (today's deadlines, pending documents, overdue tasks), staff workload overview, client health summary, revenue snapshot (MRR, outstanding invoices), AI agent activity summary.
Accessible Modules: All firm-level modules — Dashboard, Clients, Compliance Calendar, Staff & Tasks, Documents, Billing, AI Drafts, WhatsApp Console, Analytics, Settings, Client Portal Management.
Security Access: Firm administrator. MFA strongly encouraged. Session timeout at 8 hours.
Data Visibility: All data within their firm. Personal data of clients and staff within their firm.

Role 3: CA Staff (Senior) (Firm Level)
Who They Are: Qualified CA employees or senior associates working in the firm, typically handling independent client portfolios and review work.
Permissions:

View and update compliance tasks assigned to them and their team
View and update client records for assigned clients
Upload and manage documents for assigned clients
Use AI draft generator for assigned clients
Log billing time entries for their work
View team-level compliance calendar
Add comments and notes to tasks
Trigger WhatsApp document requests for assigned clients (subject to approval workflow if configured)
View client portal activity for assigned clients
Generate standard reports for assigned clients

Restrictions:

Cannot access billing invoice generation (can only log time entries)
Cannot add or delete client records
Cannot add or remove team members
Cannot modify firm-level settings
Cannot view other staff members' performance metrics unless designated as team lead
Cannot access firm-level financial summary

Dashboard: Staff Workspace — My tasks today, my deadlines this week, client document status for assigned clients, recent AI drafts, pending review items.
Accessible Modules: Dashboard (restricted), Clients (assigned only), Compliance Tasks (assigned), Documents (assigned clients), AI Drafts, Billing (time log only), Notifications.
Security Access: Standard firm employee access. MFA optional (firm-configurable as mandatory). Session timeout at 8 hours.
Data Visibility: Clients explicitly assigned to them. Team-level compliance calendar. Their own billing time entries.

Role 4: Articleship Student / Junior Staff (Firm Level)
Who They Are: Articleship students registered with ICAI and working in the CA firm, or junior staff members with limited autonomy.
Permissions:

View and update tasks explicitly assigned to them
Upload documents for tasks assigned to them
Add notes and comments to assigned tasks
Log time entries for their assigned work
View client contact information for clients they are assigned to work on
Use basic AI draft generator (with firm-owner review flag enabled)

Restrictions:

Cannot view the full client list — only see clients on tasks assigned to them
Cannot send WhatsApp messages to clients directly
Cannot access billing invoices or invoice generation
Cannot view firm-level analytics
Cannot modify any client records
Cannot access the client portal administration
All AI drafts are flagged for senior review before sending
Cannot approve their own time entries — requires senior CA review

Dashboard: My Work View — Today's assigned tasks, document upload queue, completed this week, pending approvals from senior.
Accessible Modules: Tasks (assigned only), Documents (upload only for assigned tasks), Time Log, Notifications.
Security Access: Most restricted firm user. Strong password required. Session timeout at 4 hours.
Data Visibility: Only data on tasks explicitly assigned to them. Cannot see the full client database.

Role 5: Client (Portal Level)
Who They Are: Business owners, company directors, or authorized representatives of businesses that are clients of the CA firm. They access CA-OS only through the Client Portal — a separate interface from the CA firm's internal dashboard.
Permissions:

View their own compliance status (what has been filed, what is pending, upcoming deadlines)
Upload documents requested by their CA firm
View and download completed returns and documents
View and pay invoices from their CA firm
Communicate with their CA firm via the portal messaging system
Update their own basic profile information (contact details, alternate email)
View their compliance history (last 3 years)

Restrictions:

Cannot view any other client's data
Cannot view the CA firm's internal task assignments or staff information
Cannot view billing information of other clients
Cannot contact the platform (CA-OS) directly — all communication is through their CA firm
Cannot modify compliance tasks or filing statuses
Cannot access any firm-internal settings
Cannot see AI agent logs or internal communication between CA staff

Dashboard: Client Compliance Home — Compliance health score, upcoming deadlines, document requests pending, recent filings, unpaid invoices, communication thread.
Accessible Modules: My Compliance Status, Document Upload, Invoices & Payments, Messages, My Profile.
Security Access: OTP-based login (mobile number or email). No password required. Session timeout at 2 hours. Device fingerprinting for suspicious access detection.
Data Visibility: Only their own firm's compliance data as presented by their CA firm. No raw system data.

Role 6: AI Agent (System Level)
Who They Are: Autonomous AI agents are treated as a distinct actor type in the system. Every action an AI agent takes is logged under this role identity, ensuring full traceability and auditability of automated behavior.
Permissions:

Read client compliance calendar data
Read document submission status
Send pre-approved WhatsApp template messages to clients
Create and update compliance task statuses based on trigger conditions
Generate AI drafts (stored for human review — not sent automatically unless configured)
Log actions in the AI audit trail
Trigger background jobs for document processing
Read conversation history for context

Restrictions:

Cannot modify billing records
Cannot add or delete client records
Cannot send custom (non-template) WhatsApp messages to clients without human approval (unless explicitly enabled by firm owner)
Cannot access documents that belong to firms where the AI integration is disabled
Cannot execute financial transactions
Every AI action is timestamped and attributed in audit logs
Rate-limited to prevent runaway agent loops

Dashboard: AI agents do not have a user-facing dashboard. Their activity is visible in the AI Activity Log accessible to Firm Owners.
Accessible Modules: System-internal access to compliance engine, notification service, document service, and conversation service. No UI access.
Security Access: System-level service token authentication. Token rotated every 24 hours. All actions require valid firm context.
Data Visibility: Firm-scoped. An agent operating on behalf of Firm A cannot access Firm B's data.

Role 7: Support Agent (Platform Level)
Who They Are: CA-OS customer support team members who handle inbound support tickets, onboarding assistance, and technical issues for CA firm customers.
Permissions:

View firm account details (subscription plan, team size, client count)
View recent error logs for a specific firm (when investigating a reported issue)
Create and update support tickets
Trigger password reset for firm users (with documented reason)
View WhatsApp integration status and recent message logs for troubleshooting
Access firm's billing history (read-only, for resolving billing disputes)

Restrictions:

Cannot view client financial data or document contents
Cannot make changes to any firm's data
Cannot access AI conversation logs unless specifically authorized for a support case
Cannot access Super Admin functions
All support actions are logged with ticket reference
Session timeout at 4 hours

Dashboard: Support Console — Active tickets, firm health status, recent error reports, escalation queue.
Accessible Modules: Support Ticket System, Firm Lookup (read-only, restricted view), Billing Lookup (read-only), Error Log Viewer.

Role 8: Platform Analyst (Platform Level)
Who They Are: CA-OS internal business intelligence and product analytics team members who need access to aggregated data for product decisions.
Permissions:

Access aggregated, anonymized usage analytics across all firms
View product metrics — feature adoption, engagement rates, churn signals
Access revenue analytics
Generate and export analytics reports

Restrictions:

Cannot access any personally identifiable information (PII)
Cannot access individual firm's data
All queries against the production database are proxied through the analytics layer
Cannot modify any data

Dashboard: Analytics Workspace — Product metrics, revenue dashboards, AI usage statistics, feature adoption funnels.

2.3 Role Assignment Rules

Every user belongs to exactly one Firm (except Super Admin, Support Agent, and Platform Analyst who are platform-level)
A user can have only one role within a firm
Role changes require Firm Owner authorization and are logged in the audit trail
Deactivated users lose all access immediately — their data remains but they cannot log in
The first user who creates a firm account is automatically assigned the Firm Owner role



SECTION 3: COMPLETE FRONTEND REQUIREMENTS

3.1 Design Philosophy and System
3.1.1 Overall Design Language
CA-OS follows a Modern Professional Minimalism design philosophy — clean, efficient, and trustworthy. Given the target audience (Chartered Accountants who deal with precision and financial data daily), the design must communicate reliability, clarity, and competence. It is not playful. It is not flashy. It is purposefully organized, information-dense where needed, and never overwhelming.
The design system is closest to Linear.app meets Notion meets a enterprise-grade Indian fintech — structured, functional, with subtle depth and purposeful color usage.
No glassmorphism. No heavy neumorphism. Subtle shadows for elevation (not decoration). Clean borders over shadow-heavy designs for data-dense views.
3.1.2 Typography
Primary Font: Inter (Google Fonts) — chosen for its exceptional readability in data-dense interfaces, wide language support, and professional character.
Secondary Font: JetBrains Mono — used exclusively for displaying codes, GST numbers, PAN numbers, GSTIN values, and numerical financial data where character disambiguation matters.
Font Scale:

Display: 36px / 700 weight — Used only for marketing pages
Heading 1: 28px / 600 weight — Page titles
Heading 2: 22px / 600 weight — Section headers
Heading 3: 18px / 500 weight — Card titles, modal headers
Heading 4: 15px / 500 weight — Sub-section labels, table column headers
Body Large: 15px / 400 weight — Primary reading text
Body: 14px / 400 weight — Standard interface text
Body Small: 13px / 400 weight — Secondary labels, captions
Caption: 12px / 400 weight — Timestamps, meta information
Mono: 13px / JetBrains Mono — GSTIN, PAN, financial codes

Line Height: 1.5 for body text. 1.2 for headings. 1.6 for longer form content.
Letter Spacing: 0em for body. -0.01em for headings. 0.05em for all-caps labels.
3.1.3 Color Palette
Brand Colors:

Primary: #2563EB (Blue 600) — Main actions, links, active states
Primary Dark: #1D4ED8 (Blue 700) — Primary hover states
Primary Light: #EFF6FF (Blue 50) — Primary backgrounds, highlighted rows

Neutral Palette:

Gray 950: #0C0A09 — Darkest text (dark mode backgrounds)
Gray 900: #1C1917 — Dark text, dark mode surfaces
Gray 800: #292524 — Primary text (light mode)
Gray 700: #44403C — Secondary text
Gray 600: #57534E — Tertiary text, icons
Gray 500: #78716C — Placeholder text, disabled states
Gray 400: #A8A29E — Borders (light mode), dividers
Gray 300: #D6D3D1 — Input borders, table borders
Gray 200: #E7E5E4 — Background dividers
Gray 100: #F5F5F4 — Alternate row backgrounds, subtle backgrounds
Gray 50: #FAFAF9 — Page background (light mode)
White: #FFFFFF — Card surfaces, modal backgrounds

Semantic Colors:

Success: #16A34A (Green 600) — Completed tasks, filed returns, paid invoices
Success Light: #F0FDF4 — Success backgrounds
Warning: #D97706 (Amber 600) — Upcoming deadlines (3–7 days), pending documents
Warning Light: #FFFBEB — Warning backgrounds
Danger: #DC2626 (Red 600) — Overdue tasks, missed deadlines, unpaid overdue invoices
Danger Light: #FEF2F2 — Danger backgrounds
Info: #0891B2 (Cyan 600) — Informational notices, AI suggestions
Info Light: #ECFEFF — Info backgrounds

Dark Mode Counterparts:

Background: #0C0A09
Surface: #1C1917
Card: #292524
Border: #44403C
Text Primary: #FAFAF9
Text Secondary: #D6D3D1
Text Tertiary: #A8A29E

3.1.4 Spacing System
Based on a 4px base unit. All spacing values are multiples of 4.

2px: space-0.5 — micro gaps
4px: space-1 — icon padding, tight labels
8px: space-2 — compact element padding
12px: space-3 — standard element padding
16px: space-4 — default component padding
20px: space-5 — medium section padding
24px: space-6 — card padding, section gaps
32px: space-8 — large section gaps
40px: space-10 — page section spacing
48px: space-12 — major section divisions
64px: space-16 — page-level spacing

3.1.5 Grid System
12-column grid with responsive gutters:

Mobile (320px–767px): 4 columns, 16px gutter, 16px margin
Tablet (768px–1023px): 8 columns, 20px gutter, 24px margin
Desktop (1024px–1279px): 12 columns, 24px gutter, 32px margin
Wide (1280px+): 12 columns, 24px gutter, auto margin (content max-width: 1440px)

3.1.6 Responsive Breakpoints

xs: 320px (minimum supported)
sm: 480px
md: 768px (tablet portrait)
lg: 1024px (tablet landscape / small laptop)
xl: 1280px (standard desktop)
2xl: 1536px (large monitor)

3.1.7 Component Library
Built on Radix UI primitives with a custom design system layer. Radix provides accessible, unstyled primitives (Dialog, Select, Dropdown, Tooltip, etc.) which are styled according to the CA-OS design system.
Animation library: Framer Motion for page transitions, modal animations, skeleton loading states, and micro-interactions.
Chart library: Recharts for all data visualizations — compliance health charts, revenue trend lines, workload distribution pie charts.
Data table: TanStack Table (React Table v8) — virtual scrolling for large client lists, column resizing, sorting, and filtering.
Form library: React Hook Form with Zod for schema validation on the frontend.
3.1.8 Icon System
Lucide Icons — consistent, outline-style icon set. All icons rendered at standardized sizes:

16px: Inline icons within text, input field icons
20px: Button icons, navigation icons
24px: Card header icons, section icons
32px: Empty state icons
48px: Illustration-level icons (used sparingly)

No custom icon designs in v1. Consistent Lucide usage throughout.
3.1.9 Card Design
Standard Card:

Background: White (light) / Gray 800 (dark)
Border: 1px solid Gray 200 (light) / Gray 700 (dark)
Border radius: 12px
Shadow: 0 1px 3px rgba(0,0,0,0.06) — subtle elevation only
Padding: 24px

Metric Card (for dashboard KPIs):

Same as standard card
Large metric number displayed in 32px / 700 weight
Trend indicator (up/down with color)
Sparkline or progress bar where applicable

Compliance Task Card:

Left border accent (4px) color-coded by status: Green (complete), Amber (upcoming), Red (overdue), Gray (not started)
Compact padding: 16px
Due date prominently displayed
Client name, task type, assigned staff visible at a glance

3.1.10 Motion Design
All animations use Framer Motion. Guiding principle: animations communicate state changes, not decorate interactions.

Page transitions: 200ms ease-in-out fade + 4px slide
Modal open: 150ms ease-out scale from 0.95 + fade in
Modal close: 100ms ease-in fade out
Dropdown open: 120ms ease-out, origin at trigger point
Toast notification: 300ms slide in from bottom-right, auto-dismiss at 4 seconds
Skeleton loader: continuous shimmer effect at 1.5s cycle
Success state: 200ms scale pulse on completion check
Hover states: 150ms color transitions on all interactive elements
Tab switches: 200ms slide transition between content panels

Respect prefers-reduced-motion — all animations fall back to instant transitions.
3.1.11 Skeleton Loaders
Every data-loading state has a custom skeleton that matches the final layout exactly. No generic spinners for page-level content. Spinners only for button-level actions.
Skeleton elements: Gray 200 (light) / Gray 700 (dark) base, with shimmer animation moving left to right.
3.1.12 Theme Engine
CA-OS supports three themes:

Light Mode (default)
Dark Mode — full dark mode with all components adapted
System Mode — follows OS preference

Theme preference stored in user profile on the server. Also stored in localStorage for immediate application before JS hydration.
Theme toggle accessible from user profile menu (top right corner) on every page.
3.1.13 Accessibility Standards
CA-OS targets WCAG 2.1 Level AA compliance:

All color combinations meet 4.5:1 contrast ratio for normal text
All interactive elements are focusable via keyboard
All images and icons have appropriate alt text or aria-labels
Form fields have associated labels (not just placeholder text)
Error messages are associated with their form fields via aria-describedby
Modal dialogs trap focus correctly
Toast notifications are announced via aria-live regions
All data tables have proper header associations
Screen reader tested with VoiceOver (macOS/iOS) and NVDA (Windows)
Skip-to-content link available on all pages


3.2 Global UI Components
3.2.1 Top Navigation Bar
Appears on: All authenticated pages (dashboard, clients, tasks, etc.). Does not appear on marketing pages or client portal pages.
Structure (left to right):

CA-OS logo (leftmost) — links to dashboard
Firm name display — clickable, shows firm settings dropdown
Global search bar (center) — Cmd+K / Ctrl+K keyboard shortcut activates command palette
Notification bell icon — badge count for unread notifications, opens notification drawer
AI activity indicator — subtle pulsing dot when an AI agent is actively working; clicking opens AI Activity Log
User avatar (rightmost) — opens profile dropdown: My Profile, Theme Toggle, Help & Support, Keyboard Shortcuts, Sign Out

Behavior:

Fixed position — remains visible during scroll
Height: 56px on desktop, 48px on mobile
Collapses to icon-only on mobile with hamburger menu for sidebar
Search opens a full-screen command palette with: recent clients, upcoming deadlines, quick actions, navigation shortcuts
All nav items have keyboard navigation support

Empty/Error States:

If firm name fails to load, shows "Your Firm" as fallback
If notification count fails, bell icon shows without badge

3.2.2 Left Sidebar
Appears on: All authenticated CA firm internal pages.
Navigation items (top to bottom):

Dashboard (Home icon) — Morning brief overview
Clients (Building icon) — Client list and management
Compliance (Calendar icon) — Compliance calendar and tasks
Staff & Tasks (Users icon) — Team management and Kanban board
Documents (Files icon) — Document management center
Billing (Receipt icon) — Billing logs and invoices
AI Drafts (Sparkles icon) — AI draft generator and history
WhatsApp Console (MessageCircle icon) — Bot configuration and conversation viewer
Analytics (BarChart icon) — Firm performance analytics

Bottom of sidebar:

Settings (Gear icon) — Firm and account settings
Help (CircleHelp icon) — Opens help center
Upgrade badge (if on Starter/Growth plan) — Shows upgrade prompt

Behavior:

Collapsible: can be minimized to icon-only mode (saves horizontal space on laptops)
Active state: highlighted background on the active navigation item
Hover state: subtle background on hover
Keyboard navigable: arrow keys move between items
On mobile: sidebar is off-canvas (slides in from left, overlay on main content)
Width: 240px expanded, 56px collapsed
Collapse toggle: small arrow button at the bottom of the sidebar

Empty State:

N/A — sidebar always has navigation items

3.2.3 Command Palette (Global Search)
Trigger: Cmd+K (Mac) / Ctrl+K (Windows/Linux) or clicking the search bar in top nav.
Behavior:

Full-screen overlay with centered search input
Real-time search results as user types (debounced 200ms)
Search across: client names, GSTIN, PAN, task titles, document names
Quick action shortcuts: "Add client", "Create task", "Generate AI draft", "View today's deadlines"
Recent items shown when search is empty
Keyboard navigation: arrow keys to move between results, Enter to navigate, Escape to close
Results grouped by type: Clients, Tasks, Documents, Actions


3.3 Page-by-Page Requirements
PAGE 1: Landing / Marketing Page
URL: https://caos.in/ (public, unauthenticated)
Purpose: Convert CA professionals visiting the website into free trial signups. Communicate the value proposition clearly and create urgency.
Layout Structure:
Full-width single-page design with sticky navigation. No sidebar. Sections flow vertically.
Sections:

Hero Section: Large headline, subheadline, primary CTA button ("Start Free Trial"), secondary CTA ("Book a Demo"), hero image/illustration showing the CA-OS dashboard
Social Proof Bar: "Trusted by X CA firms in Hyderabad" + 3–4 firm logos (with permission)
Problem Section: Three-panel layout showing the chaos before CA-OS (WhatsApp screenshots, Excel mess) vs. after CA-OS
Feature Highlights: Six feature cards — Compliance Calendar, WhatsApp Bot, AI Drafts, Client Portal, Staff Tasks, Billing — each with icon, title, 2-line description
How It Works: Three-step illustration: Add your clients → Set up compliance calendar → Let AI handle the rest
Testimonials: Carousel of 4–5 testimonials from beta CA users, with name, firm name, city
Pricing Section: Three pricing tiers with feature comparison table
FAQ Section: Accordion with 8–10 common questions
Final CTA Section: "Join 500+ CA firms on CA-OS" with signup form
Footer: Links, legal, social media

User Interactions:

Pricing toggle between monthly and annual billing (shows savings)
FAQ accordion expand/collapse
Testimonial carousel auto-plays, pausable on hover
Sticky nav shows "Sign In" and "Start Free Trial" buttons

Animations:

Hero section elements fade in sequentially on load (staggered 150ms delays)
Feature cards animate in on scroll (intersection observer triggered)
Statistics counter animation (numbers count up when in viewport)

Mobile Responsiveness:

Single-column layout on mobile
Navigation collapses to hamburger menu
Hero image hidden on mobile (text only)
Pricing cards stack vertically


PAGE 2: Signup / Registration (Firm Onboarding)
URL: https://caos.in/signup
Purpose: Allow a new CA to create a firm account on CA-OS. The process must be fast (under 3 minutes), low-friction, and immediately useful after completion.
Layout Structure:
Centered card on a branded background. No sidebar. Progress indicator showing 3 steps.
Step 1 — Basic Details:

Mobile number (required) — used for OTP and WhatsApp
Full name (required)
Email address (required)
ICAI Membership Number (optional — for verification badge)
"I agree to Terms & Conditions and Privacy Policy" checkbox

Step 2 — Firm Details:

Firm name (required)
Firm type: Sole Practitioner / Partnership Firm / LLP / Company
City (dropdown — Indian cities)
State (auto-populated based on city, editable)
Primary GST specialization (multi-select): GST Compliance / Income Tax / Audit / International Tax / Other
Number of clients (range: Under 20 / 20–50 / 50–100 / 100–200 / 200+) — used for plan recommendation

Step 3 — OTP Verification + Plan Selection:

6-digit OTP sent to mobile number
OTP input field (auto-advance between boxes)
Resend OTP option (available after 30 seconds)
Post-verification: plan recommendation shown based on client count
Option to start 14-day free trial of Professional plan

Form Validation Behavior:

Real-time validation on field blur (not on keystroke)
Mobile number: validate Indian format (10 digits, starts with 6–9)
ICAI number: validate format (3 letters + 6 digits)
Email: standard email format validation
Firm name: minimum 3 characters, no special characters except '&' and '.'
Error messages appear below the field in red, with an icon

Loading States:

OTP send: button shows spinner and "Sending OTP..." text
After OTP verification: full-page loader with "Setting up your workspace..." message and animated progress bar

Error States:

Invalid OTP: red border on OTP input, "Incorrect OTP. X attempts remaining." message
OTP expired: "OTP has expired. Please request a new one." with resend button
Server error: Toast notification "Something went wrong. Please try again." with support link

Success State:
After successful account creation → redirect to onboarding wizard (Page 3)
Empty States:
N/A — form fields have placeholders guiding the user
Mobile Responsiveness:

Identical experience on mobile — same form, slightly adjusted padding
OTP input fields large enough for finger tapping (min 44px touch targets)


PAGE 3: Onboarding Wizard
URL: https://caos.in/onboarding
Purpose: Guide a newly registered CA through the essential setup steps so they get immediate value from CA-OS within the first session. The wizard is skippable (with a warning) but strongly encouraged.
Layout: Full-screen wizard with left panel showing progress checklist and right panel showing the current step.
Steps:
Step 1 — Welcome & Context:

Personalized welcome ("Welcome, CA [Name]! Let's set up your CA-OS workspace.")
Estimated time: "Takes about 5 minutes"
What they'll accomplish: add first client, see their compliance calendar, configure WhatsApp

Step 2 — Add First Client:

Simplified client add form (not the full form — just essentials)
Company/individual name
GSTIN (with auto-validation via GST API lookup)
PAN
Primary mobile number
Compliance services needed: GST Monthly / TDS Quarterly / ITR Annual / Audit
After save, system shows: "Done! CA-OS has generated 47 compliance tasks for this client for the next 12 months."

Step 3 — Connect WhatsApp:

Explanation of how WhatsApp integration works
Option 1: Use the CA-OS shared WhatsApp number (instant, no setup)
Option 2: Connect your own WhatsApp Business number via Meta Business Verification (guided flow)
Test message sent to CA's own WhatsApp: "Hi! CA-OS is now connected. Your compliance management system is ready."

Step 4 — Invite Team Member:

Optional step
Add email of one team member (CA staff or articleship student)
They receive an invitation email to join the firm on CA-OS

Step 5 — Done! Your Dashboard Awaits:

Summary of what was set up
Quick links: "View Compliance Calendar", "Add More Clients", "Explore AI Drafts"
Confetti animation (subtle, 2 seconds)
"Go to Dashboard" button

Skip Behavior:

"Skip Setup" link visible at all times
Clicking shows a modal: "Are you sure? You can always complete setup from Settings."
Skipping takes to dashboard with a persistent but dismissible setup completion banner


PAGE 4: Main Dashboard (Firm Morning Brief)
URL: https://caos.in/dashboard
Purpose: The CA's single most important daily view. Designed to be consumed in under 60 seconds each morning. Should answer: What needs my attention today? What is my firm's overall health? What is my team doing?
Layout:
Top row: 4 metric cards (full width)
Second row: 2 panels side by side (Deadlines this week | Documents Pending)
Third row: 3 panels (Staff workload | AI Activity | Recent Billing)
Bottom: Quick action bar
Component: Today's Deadline Count Card

Large number showing total deadlines due today
Color-coded: Green if 0, Amber if 1–3, Red if 4+
Subtitle: "across X clients"
Clicking navigates to Compliance Calendar filtered to today

Component: Overdue Tasks Card

Large number in red if > 0
Subtitle: "require immediate attention"
Clicking navigates to Compliance Calendar filtered to overdue

Component: Documents Pending Card

Total pending document requests sent to clients
Subtitle: "clients haven't responded yet"
Clicking navigates to Documents section filtered to pending

Component: Monthly Revenue Card

Total invoiced amount this month in ₹
Comparison to last month (% change)
Clicking navigates to Billing section

Component: This Week's Deadlines Panel (Left half, second row)

List of all compliance tasks due in the next 7 days
Each row: Client name | Task type | Due date | Assigned staff | Status
Color-coded left border by status
Sorted by due date ascending
Maximum 10 shown, "View all" link to full calendar
Filter: Today / This Week / Next Week (toggle buttons)

Component: Documents Pending Panel (Right half, second row)

List of clients with pending document requests
Each row: Client name | Document requested | Days since request | Status
"Send Reminder" quick action button on each row
Quick escalation: "Call client" button opens phone dialer via tel: protocol

Component: Staff Workload Panel (Third row, left)

Mini bar chart showing each staff member's task count
Green: within capacity, Amber: near capacity, Red: overloaded
Click on staff name → opens their task list

Component: AI Activity Panel (Third row, center)

Last 5 AI agent actions: "[Deadline Agent] Sent reminder to TechCorp Pvt Ltd for GSTR-3B"
Timestamp for each action
"View all AI activity" link

Component: Recent Billing Panel (Third row, right)

Last 5 invoice actions: created, sent, paid
Quick "Create Invoice" button

Quick Action Bar (Bottom):

Add Client button
Create Task button
Generate AI Draft button
Send Bulk Reminder button

Empty States:

If no clients added: Large illustration + "Add your first client to get started" with CTA button
If no deadlines: "You're all caught up! No deadlines in the next 7 days."
If no staff: "Add team members to track workload."

Loading State:

Each card and panel has its own skeleton loader
Data loads in parallel — cards appear as they load, not all at once

Refresh Behavior:

Dashboard data refreshes every 60 seconds automatically (no full page reload)
Manual refresh button available

Mobile Responsiveness:

All metric cards stack to 2×2 grid on tablet, 1×4 list on mobile
Second and third rows become single column on mobile
Quick action bar becomes a floating action button (FAB) with expanded menu


PAGE 5: Client List Page
URL: https://caos.in/clients
Purpose: Allow the CA to view, search, filter, and manage all their clients. The canonical record for every business the CA firm serves.
Layout:
Top bar: Page title + Add Client button + Search bar + Filter controls
Main area: Data table with client records
Pagination at bottom
Data Table Columns:

Client Name (sortable) + business type badge (Pvt Ltd / Partnership / Individual)
GSTIN (monospace font)
Compliance Services (small pill badges: GST / TDS / ITR / Audit)
Compliance Health Score (1–10 dial indicator)
Documents Pending count (badge)
Overdue Tasks count (badge, red if > 0)
Last Activity date
Assigned Staff (avatar stack, max 3 shown + count)
Actions: three-dot menu → View | Edit | Archive | Send WhatsApp

Search:

Real-time search by client name, GSTIN, PAN (debounced 300ms)
Search persists in URL params (shareable, bookmarkable)

Filters (collapsible filter panel):

Compliance Services (multi-select checkboxes)
Compliance Health (range slider 1–10)
City/State
Client Type (Company / Firm / Individual / Trust)
Assigned Staff (multi-select)
Has Pending Documents (toggle)
Has Overdue Tasks (toggle)
Date range: Date added

Sort Options:

Client Name (A–Z, Z–A)
Health Score (highest, lowest)
Overdue Tasks (most urgent first)
Recently Added

Pagination: 25 clients per page by default. User-configurable to 50 or 100. Total count shown. Jump-to-page input.
Row Interactions:

Hover: subtle row highlight
Click anywhere on row (except action buttons): opens client detail view
Clicking GSTIN: copies to clipboard with toast confirmation

Bulk Actions:

Checkbox on each row for multi-select
Bulk actions bar appears when 1+ rows selected: "Send Reminder to Selected", "Assign Staff", "Export Selected"
Select All selects current page only; "Select all X clients" option appears after selecting page

Empty State:

If no clients: illustration + "No clients yet. Add your first client to get started." + Add Client CTA
If search returns no results: "No clients found matching '[search term]'. Try a different name or GSTIN." + Clear Search link

Add Client (Modal):

Opens as a side panel (not a page) sliding in from the right
Full client creation form (see Section 3.4 for form specifications)
Save button creates client and adds to list with success toast


PAGE 6: Client Detail Page
URL: https://caos.in/clients/[clientId]
Purpose: The complete profile of a single client — their contact information, compliance status, all their tasks, all their documents, their billing history, and the communication log.
Layout:
Top: Client header (name, GSTIN, type badge, health score, quick action buttons)
Below: Tab navigation with 6 tabs
Client Header Component:

Client/Company name (large, editable inline on click)
Constitution type badge (Pvt Ltd / LLP / Proprietorship / etc.)
GSTIN displayed in monospace with copy button
PAN displayed in monospace with copy button
Compliance Health Score (large dial: 1–10)
Status indicators: overdue tasks count, pending documents count, outstanding invoice amount
Quick actions: Add Task | Request Document | Generate AI Draft | Send WhatsApp | View Portal

Tab 1: Overview

Contact details: primary contact name, mobile, email, address
Compliance services enrolled (with toggle to add/remove services)
Assigned staff members (with reassignment capability)
Recent activity timeline (last 10 actions on this client)
Notes section (rich text, free-form notes by CA)

Tab 2: Compliance

Full compliance task list for this client
Grouped by: month and year
Each task row: Task type | Due Date | Status | Assigned to | Documents ready | Actions
Status filter: All / Pending / In Progress / Completed / Overdue
"Add Custom Task" button for ad-hoc compliance items

Tab 3: Documents

Grid view of all documents related to this client
Grouped by type: Bank Statements / Invoices / GST Returns / TDS Certificates / Audit Reports / Others
Each document: thumbnail, file name, upload date, uploaded by (client or staff), status (received / processed / verified)
Upload document button (opens file picker)
Document request history: sent on [date], status [pending/received]

Tab 4: Billing

Invoice list: invoice number, date, amount, status (draft / sent / paid / overdue)
Total outstanding amount highlighted in amber/red
Time logs for this client: date, staff member, hours, description, billable amount
"Create Invoice" button
"Add Time Log" button

Tab 5: Communication

Chronological log of all WhatsApp messages, emails, and portal messages between the firm and this client
Message bubbles showing direction (sent vs received)
"Send WhatsApp" button opens compose drawer
"Send Email" button opens email compose drawer

Tab 6: AI Insights

Penalty Risk Score (1–10) with explanation
AI-generated summary of client's compliance history ("This client has missed document deadlines 3 times in the last 6 months and carries moderate penalty risk.")
Suggested actions from AI
Churn risk indicator
Billing opportunity alerts ("2 advisory sessions logged but not invoiced")


PAGE 7: Compliance Calendar Page
URL: https://caos.in/compliance
Purpose: The master view of all compliance deadlines across all clients. Allows the CA to see what is due today, this week, this month, and beyond — with full filtering and drill-down capability.
Layout:
Top: Page title + View toggle (Calendar | List | Board) + Filter bar
Main: Calendar or List view based on toggle
Calendar View:

Monthly calendar grid (standard month view)
Each date cell shows count of tasks due on that day (colored by status)
Clicking a date: expands below calendar to show all tasks for that date
Color coding: Green (completed), Amber (due within 3 days), Red (overdue), Gray (not started, due in future)
Mini legend at top

List View:

Sorted by due date ascending (soonest first)
Grouped by date (sticky date headers)
Each task row: Client Name | Task Type | Assigned To | Status | Due Date | Documents Ready? | Actions
Compact, dense layout optimized for scanning

Board View (Kanban):

Four columns: Not Started | In Progress | Review | Completed
Task cards draggable between columns
Each card: Client name, task type, due date, assigned staff avatar
Card color coded by urgency (deadline proximity)

Filters:

Date range picker (default: current month)
Return type: GSTR-1 / GSTR-3B / GSTR-9 / TDS Q1 / TDS Q2 / TDS Q3 / TDS Q4 / ITR / Audit / Custom
Status: Not Started / In Progress / Completed / Overdue
Assigned staff (multi-select)
Client (search-in-filter)

Bulk Operations:

Select multiple tasks → Bulk assign staff, Bulk update status, Bulk export

Deadline Alert Banner:

Select client

Auto-imported time logs for the period (from Time Logs)
Add additional line items (ad-hoc work)
Apply GST (18% for professional services, auto-calculated)
Preview invoice (PDF preview)

Table: Date | Staff Member | Client | Description | Hours | Rate | Amount | Billable? | Invoice #
"Add Time Log" button (quick entry form)

Manage reusable invoice line items (e.g., "Monthly GST Filing — ₹X,XXX")
Retainer fee configuration per client

GST Notice Response
Income Tax Notice Response
Client Query Response (WhatsApp/Email)
Tax Computation Letter
Penalty Waiver Request
Engagement Letter

Custom (free-form prompt)

"Generate Draft" button → spinner → AI output appears in right panel
Draft is fully editable in the right panel (rich text editor)
Actions: Copy to clipboard | Send via WhatsApp | Send via Email | Download as PDF | Save to client's document folder

Draft History:

Left panel bottom: last 20 drafts with client name, type, date
Click to reopen any previous draft


PAGE 12: WhatsApp Console Page
URL: https://caos.in/whatsapp
Purpose: Full visibility and control over the WhatsApp automation layer — configure the bot, view all conversations, send manual messages, and review the bot's performance.
Layout:
Top: Connection status indicator (WhatsApp number connected / not connected)

Right panel: Full conversation thread (selected from left)
Bottom-right: Message compose bar
Conversation List:

Search by client name
Filter: All / Bot Conversations / Human Takeover / Unread
Each conversation item: client avatar + name, last message preview (50 chars), timestamp, unread count badge
Status indicator: Bot handling (robot icon) / Human handling (person icon)

Conversation Thread:

Full message history with timestamps
Message bubbles differentiated by: Client (left, gray) / Bot (right, blue with robot label) / CA/Staff (right, blue with staff name label)
Document messages show as attachment cards (clickable to view)
AI draft suggestions appear inline when client asks a question (staff can approve and send in one click)
"Take over conversation" button switches from bot to manual mode

Bot Configuration (Settings link):

Enable/disable the bot
Configure business hours (bot active during / outside hours)
Default greeting message
Document request templates
Out-of-office message

Revenue trend line: MRR over last 12 months

Top 10 highest-revenue clients

Client Engagement:

Document submission rate by client
WhatsApp response rate by client
Portal activity heat map

PAGE 15: Client Portal (Separate Interface)
URL: https://caos.in/portal/[firmSlug]/
Purpose: The client-facing interface. A clean, simple view of the client's compliance status with their CA firm. NOT the CA firm's internal tool. Designed for non-technical business owners.
Branding: White-labeled per firm — firm's logo and firm name appear instead of CA-OS logo. The portal URL can use the firm's custom domain (Enterprise plan).
Login:

OTP-based login with mobile number or email
No password — designed for easy access by non-tech-savvy clients

Home Screen (Client Dashboard):

Greeting: "Good morning, [Contact Name]"
Compliance health summary (simple language: "Your GST filings are up to date ✅ | Your October bank statement is awaited 📋")
Upcoming deadlines (in plain language, not legal jargon)
Documents requested from you (prominent call-to-action)
Outstanding invoices

Document Upload:

Simple interface: "Upload your bank statement for October"
Drag-and-drop or file picker
Progress indicator during upload
Confirmation message on success

Compliance History:

Simple list: "GST Filing — October 2025 — Filed on 18 Oct ✅"
Downloadable filed returns (if CA has uploaded them)

Messages:

Simple messaging thread with their CA firm
Can send a question, gets response from CA

Invoices:

List of invoices: Date | Amount | Status | Pay Now button
"Pay Now" opens Razorpay checkout



SECTION 4: COMPLETE BACKEND REQUIREMENTS

4.1 Backend Architecture Philosophy
CA-OS uses a Modular Monolith architecture for v1.0, with clear module boundaries designed for eventual extraction into microservices if/when scale demands. This decision is deliberate:
Rationale for Modular Monolith over Microservices (v1.0):

Team size: 2–5 engineers initially. Microservices overhead (service discovery, distributed tracing, network hops) exceeds the team's capacity to manage.
Data model: Several modules share transactional data (compliance task completion → billing log creation → notification send). Distributed transactions across microservices would introduce complexity without benefit at this scale.
Debugging simplicity: A modular monolith is significantly easier to debug, trace, and monitor with a small team.
Migration path: Clear module interfaces with no shared database tables between modules allows extraction to microservices at Year 2 when scale demands it.

Module Boundaries:
Each module is a self-contained Python package with its own models, services, routers, and tests. Modules communicate through well-defined service interfaces, not direct database queries across module boundaries.
Modules:

auth — Authentication and session management
firms — Firm management and settings
clients — Client records and profiles
compliance — Compliance calendar, tasks, GST deadline engine
documents — Document storage, OCR, extraction
staff — Team management and task assignment
billing — Billing logs, invoice generation, Razorpay integration
ai — AI agent orchestration, draft generation, LLM calls
whatsapp — WhatsApp webhook handling, message sending, bot logic
portal — Client portal authentication and data serving
notifications — Email, SMS, and in-app notification delivery
analytics — Firm analytics data aggregation and serving
admin — Platform admin operations


4.2 Framework and Language
Language: Python 3.11+
Framework: FastAPI — chosen for:

Async-first design (critical for I/O-heavy workloads: LLM calls, WhatsApp API calls, file processing)
Automatic OpenAPI documentation generation
Pydantic integration (request/response validation, type safety)
Strong ecosystem for AI/ML integration
Performance: significantly faster than Flask/Django for concurrent async workloads

ASGI Server: Uvicorn with multiple workers (number of workers = CPU cores × 2 + 1)
Process Manager: Gunicorn managing Uvicorn workers for production

4.3 API Gateway
No dedicated API gateway service in v1.0. FastAPI handles all routing. A reverse proxy (Nginx) sits in front of FastAPI and handles:

SSL termination
Static file serving
Request routing (API traffic to FastAPI, static assets to CDN)
Rate limiting at the network level (using Nginx rate limiting module)
IP-based blocking (from security incidents)

v2.0 consideration: If microservices are adopted, a dedicated API gateway (Kong or AWS API Gateway) will be introduced.

4.4 Queue System and Background Workers
Message Broker: Redis (via Upstash for managed Redis)
Task Queue: Celery with Redis as broker and result backend
Why Celery: Mature, well-tested Python task queue with excellent FastAPI integration, scheduling (Celery Beat), retry management, and monitoring (Flower dashboard).
Queue Types:
high_priority_queue — Tasks that must execute within seconds:

WhatsApp message delivery (user is waiting for acknowledgment)
OTP delivery
Real-time notification dispatch

standard_queue — Tasks that should complete within minutes:

Document OCR processing
AI draft generation
Compliance calendar updates after client modification

low_priority_queue — Tasks that can wait up to hours:

Analytics data aggregation
Monthly invoice auto-generation
Churn prediction analysis
Bulk email sending

scheduled_queue — Celery Beat tasks on time-based schedules:

Deadline reminders (every 6 hours)
Daily morning brief generation (6 AM IST)
Monthly invoice creation (1st of month, 8 AM IST)
Weekly churn analysis (Sunday midnight IST)

Dead Letter Queue: Failed tasks that exceed max retry attempts are moved to a dead letter queue stored in Redis. Super Admin alert triggered. Manual review and requeue possible from Admin Panel.
Retry Strategy:

All tasks: 3 retry attempts maximum
Retry delay: exponential backoff starting at 60 seconds (60s, 120s, 240s)
WhatsApp delivery retries: 3 attempts at 30-second intervals before fallback to SMS


4.5 Rate Limiting
Application-Level Rate Limiting (via slowapi library):
Authentication endpoints:

/auth/login: 5 requests per minute per IP
/auth/verify-otp: 5 requests per minute per IP
/auth/resend-otp: 2 requests per 5 minutes per IP

API endpoints (authenticated):

Standard API endpoints: 100 requests per minute per user
AI draft generation: 10 requests per minute per firm (to control LLM costs)
Document upload: 20 requests per minute per firm
WhatsApp send: 30 messages per minute per firm (Meta API limits observed)
Bulk operations (send reminder to 50+ clients): 2 requests per minute per firm

Network-Level Rate Limiting (Nginx):

General: 1,000 requests per minute per IP before connection throttling
Login page: 10 requests per minute per IP

Rate Limit Response: HTTP 429 with Retry-After header and a JSON body: {"error": "rate_limited", "retry_after": 60, "message": "Too many requests. Please wait 60 seconds."}

4.6 WebSocket Support and Real-Time Architecture
Use Cases for Real-Time:

Dashboard data refresh (deadline counts, document status updates)
Kanban board task drag-and-drop sync across multiple staff members
WhatsApp console new message arrival
AI draft generation progress indicator

Implementation:
FastAPI's native WebSocket support is used for real-time connections.
WebSocket Architecture:

Connection endpoint: wss://api.caos.in/ws/[firmId]
Authentication: JWT token passed as a query parameter on connection initiation
Connection validated on server before accepting
Each connected client subscribes to a firm-scoped channel
Server pushes events to all connected users of the same firm

Events pushed via WebSocket:

task.status_changed — triggers dashboard count refresh and Kanban board update
document.received — triggers document pending count refresh
whatsapp.message_received — new message in WhatsApp console
ai.draft_ready — AI draft generation complete
invoice.paid — payment confirmed

Scaling WebSockets: Redis Pub/Sub is used for WebSocket message broadcasting across multiple server instances. When a task is completed on Server A, it publishes to Redis. All server instances subscribe and push to their connected clients.
Fallback: If WebSocket connection is unavailable, frontend falls back to polling every 30 seconds.

4.7 Caching Architecture
Caching Tool: Redis (Upstash managed)
What is Cached and for How Long:
DataCache DurationCache Key PatternInvalidation TriggerGSTIN validation result (from GST API)24 hoursgstin:validate:{gstin}Manual clear onlyIndian GST deadline calendar30 dayscompliance:calendar:{year}:{month}Updated when CBIC notification detectedUser session data8 hourssession:{userId}Logout or password changeFirm settings15 minutesfirm:settings:{firmId}Firm settings updateDashboard metrics (per firm)5 minutesdashboard:{firmId}:{metric}Underlying data change eventsClient list (per firm)5 minutesclients:list:{firmId}:{page}:{filters}Client add/edit/archiveAI draft templates1 hourai:templates:{type}Template update in adminHoliday calendar (India)365 daysholidays:{year}Annual refresh
Cache-Aside Pattern: Application code always checks cache first. On cache miss, fetches from database, stores in cache, returns result. On data mutation, relevant cache keys are invalidated.
Redis Data Structures Used:

STRING: Simple key-value caching for most data
HASH: Session data (multiple fields per session)
SORTED SET: Notification queues ordered by priority
PUB/SUB: WebSocket event broadcasting
LIST: Dead letter queue


4.8 File Storage Architecture
Primary Storage: AWS S3 (ap-south-1 region — Mumbai)
Bucket Organization:

caos-documents-prod — Client-uploaded documents and CA-uploaded files (production)
caos-documents-staging — Staging environment
caos-exports-prod — Generated exports (CSV, PDF invoices)
caos-ai-temp-prod — Temporary files for AI processing (auto-deleted after 24 hours via lifecycle policy)

Access Patterns:

All S3 URLs are pre-signed with 1-hour expiry for downloads
No direct S3 URL exposure — all file access goes through the API which generates signed URLs
Upload: frontend requests a pre-signed upload URL from the API → uploads directly to S3 → notifies API of completion
This pattern avoids file data passing through the API server (critical for performance and bandwidth)

CDN: AWS CloudFront in front of the caos-documents-prod bucket for faster download delivery across India.
Virus Scanning: ClamAV integrated via a Lambda function triggered on S3 upload. Files are quarantined in a separate bucket if malware is detected. CA is notified if a client-uploaded file fails virus scan.

4.9 Search Architecture
Search Use Cases:

Client search by name, GSTIN, PAN
Task search by description
Document search by file name
Global command palette search

v1.0 Solution: PostgreSQL full-text search using tsvector and tsquery. Fast enough for the data volumes at v1.0 scale (up to 50,000 clients across all firms).
Implementation:

clients table has a search_vector column of type tsvector, updated via trigger on name, GSTIN, PAN changes
GIN index on search_vector for efficient full-text queries
websearch_to_tsquery used for user-friendly search input handling

v2.0 Migration Plan: Migrate to Elasticsearch (AWS OpenSearch) when client records exceed 500,000 and complex faceted search is required.

4.10 AI Inference Pipeline
Detailed in Section 9 (AI/LLM Requirements). Summary:

LLM API calls are asynchronous — never block the request-response cycle
All LLM calls go through the AIService class (centralized, manageable, testable)
LLM responses are cached where possible (exact same prompt → cached response, 1-hour TTL)
Token usage is tracked per firm and per AI feature for cost management
Streaming responses from Claude API are used for the AI draft generator (user sees text appearing progressively)


4.11 Logging Architecture
Structured Logging: All logs output as JSON (not plain text) for easy ingestion by log aggregation systems.
Log Fields (every log entry):

timestamp: ISO 8601
level: DEBUG / INFO / WARNING / ERROR / CRITICAL
service: module name (e.g., compliance, ai, whatsapp)
firm_id: firm context (null for platform-level logs)
user_id: user context (null for system operations)
request_id: unique ID per HTTP request (for correlation)
message: human-readable description
data: structured additional data (context-dependent)
duration_ms: for request logs, the processing duration

Log Levels:

DEBUG: Verbose development-only logs (disabled in production)
INFO: Normal operational events (request received, task queued, AI call made)
WARNING: Unexpected but recoverable conditions (cache miss, retry attempt, validation failure)
ERROR: Failures that require attention (API call failed, LLM call error, database connection issue)
CRITICAL: System-threatening conditions (database down, all queue workers dead)

Log Destinations:

Application logs: AWS CloudWatch Logs (structured JSON)
Access logs: Nginx → CloudWatch Logs
Background task logs: Celery → CloudWatch Logs

Log Retention:

INFO and below: 30 days
WARNING and above: 90 days
Security-related logs: 1 year (compliance requirement)
Audit logs (user actions): 3 years (compliance requirement)


4.12 Audit Trails
Every user-initiated action that modifies data creates an immutable audit log entry.
Audit Log Entry Structure:
audit_id: UUID
timestamp: ISO 8601
firm_id: UUID
actor_id: UUID (user or AI agent performing the action)
actor_role: enum (firm_owner / ca_staff / articleship / client / ai_agent / super_admin)
action_type: enum (create / update / delete / login / logout / export / send_message / etc.)
resource_type: enum (client / compliance_task / document / invoice / staff_member / etc.)
resource_id: UUID
changes_json: JSON (before/after values for updates)
ip_address: string
user_agent: string
request_id: UUID (correlates to application logs)
Audit logs are:

Written to a separate audit_log table
Never updated or deleted (append-only)
Accessible by Firm Owner for their own firm's data
Accessible by Super Admin for any firm

Audit Trail Coverage:

All client record changes
All compliance task status changes
All document uploads and deletions
All invoice creations, modifications, and sends
All staff additions, role changes, and deactivations
All WhatsApp message sends
All AI draft generations
All logins and logouts
All settings changes
All data exports



SECTION 5: DATABASE DESIGN REQUIREMENTS

5.1 Database Technology Decision
Primary Database: PostgreSQL 15+ (via Supabase managed PostgreSQL)
Why PostgreSQL:

Rich JSONB support for semi-structured data (client compliance settings, AI extraction results, WhatsApp message logs)
Full-text search via tsvector (avoids a separate search service for v1.0)
Row-Level Security (RLS) — critical for multi-tenancy isolation
Strong ACID compliance for financial data (billing, invoices)
Excellent Python ORM support (SQLAlchemy async)
Supabase provides managed PostgreSQL with point-in-time recovery, automatic backups, and built-in connection pooling (PgBouncer)

Secondary Storage:

Redis (Upstash): Session storage, caching, Celery broker
AWS S3: File/document blob storage
AWS CloudWatch Logs: Log storage

Analytics Storage: A separate read-only PostgreSQL replica is used for analytics queries to avoid impacting production performance.

5.2 Multi-Tenancy Architecture
CA-OS uses a Single Database, Single Schema, Shared Tables multi-tenancy model with PostgreSQL Row-Level Security (RLS).
Why this approach:

Simplest to build and maintain for a small team
PostgreSQL RLS provides strong isolation guarantees at the database level
All tables include a firm_id foreign key
RLS policies enforce that queries can only access rows where firm_id matches the authenticated user's firm context

Alternative considered: Schema-per-tenant (one PostgreSQL schema per firm). Rejected because schema creation/migration across thousands of schemas is operationally complex.
RLS Policy Example:
Every table has an RLS policy that checks firm_id = current_setting('app.firm_id')::uuid. The application sets this configuration parameter at the start of each database connection using the authenticated user's firm context.

5.3 Naming Conventions

Table names: snake_case, plural (e.g., compliance_tasks, client_documents)
Column names: snake_case (e.g., created_at, firm_id)
Primary key: always id UUID DEFAULT gen_random_uuid()
Foreign keys: referenced_table_singular_id (e.g., firm_id, client_id)
Audit columns: every table has created_at TIMESTAMPTZ, updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ, created_by UUID, updated_by UUID
Soft delete: deleted_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL (null = active, timestamp = deleted)
