# 27. DATA MIGRATION STRATEGY

## 27.1 Client Data Migration — CSV Import

**CA-OS provides downloadable Excel template with columns:**
`Client Name | GSTIN | PAN | TAN | CIN | Contact Name | Mobile | Email | City | State | Services (comma-sep) | GST Frequency | Retainer Fee | Primary Staff (email) | Notes`

**Max 500 clients per CSV upload.**

**Import processing:**
1. Validate CSV structure (correct columns present)
2. Row-by-row validation — invalid rows logged with reason, not imported
3. GSTIN format validation (local checksum, no API call for bulk)
4. Duplicate check: If GSTIN or name already exists, flag for review
5. Bulk insert valid rows in single transaction
6. Queue compliance calendar generation for all imported clients (background)
7. Return import report: `{total, imported, failed, failure_reasons[]}`

**Assisted migration (Professional/Enterprise):** CA-OS Customer Success team migrates firms with 50+ clients. CA exports from current tool in any format; CS team reformats and imports via admin import tool. Included in Professional/Enterprise onboarding.

## 27.2 Compliance History Migration

Historical filing records can be imported to pre-populate health scores.

**CSV format:** `Client GSTIN | Return Type | Period (YYYY-MM) | Filed Date | ARN | Filed On Time (Y/N)`

Imported records create `compliance_tasks` with `status = "filed"` and the provided reference. These populate compliance history and health score calculation.

## 27.3 Database Schema Migration (Ongoing)

**Tool:** Alembic

**Golden Rules:**
1. Every schema change through a migration file — no manual DB changes ever
2. Every migration must be reversible (`upgrade()` and `downgrade()` both implemented)
3. Migrations removing data (DROP TABLE/COLUMN) require a 3-step deployment
4. All migrations tested on staging before production

**Zero-downtime migration for large tables:**
1. Add column as nullable (instant, no table lock)
2. Backfill in batches of 1,000 via Celery task
3. Add NOT NULL constraint after backfill complete (separate migration)
4. Never add NOT NULL column with no default to a table with existing rows in one step

# 29. VENDOR RISK REGISTER

## 29.1 Critical Vendor Dependencies

| Vendor | Criticality | Risk Level | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Anthropic (Claude API) | Very High | Medium | OpenAI GPT-4o fallback configured; AI features degrade gracefully |
| Meta (WhatsApp API) | Very High | Medium | SMS fallback (MSG91); email fallback; in-app always available |
| Supabase (PostgreSQL) | Critical | Low | PITR; cross-region S3 backup; AWS RDS migration documented |
| Upstash (Redis) | High | Low | App runs without cache (degraded perf); DB fallback for sessions |
| Razorpay | High | Low | Switch to PayU or Cashfree; manual payment links as emergency |
| AWS | High | Very Low | Multi-AZ within India; DR plan to Singapore region |
| Vercel | Medium | Low | Next.js deployable on EC2 or Netlify with config changes only |
| MSG91 | Medium | Low | Switch to Twilio or AWS SNS for SMS |
| Resend | Low | Very Low | Switchable to AWS SES in 1 hour |
| Google Document AI | Medium | Low | AWS Textract is drop-in alternative; Tesseract as degraded fallback |

## 29.2 Vendor Lock-In Assessment

**High Lock-In: Supabase**  
Mitigation: SQLAlchemy ORM means application code is database-agnostic. Schema is portable to any PostgreSQL instance. Supabase RLS policies recreatable as application-level checks if migrating. Migration path documented.

**Medium Lock-In: Vercel**  
Mitigation: Next.js is not Vercel-specific. Deployable on AWS Amplify, Netlify, or EC2 (with PM2) with only environment configuration changes.

**Low Lock-In: Anthropic**  
Mitigation: AIService class abstracts the LLM provider. All API calls go through this class. Provider switch requires changes in one file only (model names, client library).

# 30. GLOSSARY OF TERMS

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| ARN | Acknowledgment Reference Number — issued by GST portal on successful filing |
| AATO | Aggregate Annual Turnover — determines GST filing obligations and e-invoicing applicability |
| Articleship | 3-year mandatory practical training period for CA students, working in CA firms |
| Compliance Task | A single filing deliverable (e.g., GSTR-1 for October 2025 for a specific client) |
| Compliance Calendar | Complete schedule of all deadlines for a client across all registered compliance services |
| Compliance Health Score | 1–10 score per client based on on-time filing rate, document submission rate, and penalty history |
| DPDPA | Digital Personal Data Protection Act 2023 — India's primary data privacy law |
| DLT | Distributed Ledger Technology — mandatory platform registration for commercial SMS in India since 2021 |
| Firm | The CA firm account — the primary tenant unit in CA-OS |
| GSTIN | Goods and Services Tax Identification Number — unique 15-character identifier for GST-registered businesses |
| GST Portal | https://www.gst.gov.in — the Indian government portal for GST filings |
| GSTR-1 | Monthly/quarterly outward supply details return |
| GSTR-3B | Monthly/quarterly summary return with tax payment |
| GSTR-9 | Annual GST return |
| IFF | Invoice Furnishing Facility — mechanism for QRMP taxpayers to upload invoices monthly |
| IRN | Invoice Reference Number — issued by the e-invoice portal (IRP) for valid e-invoices |
| IRP | Invoice Registration Portal — government platform for e-invoice generation |
| MRR | Monthly Recurring Revenue — CA-OS's primary revenue metric |
| OTP | One-Time Password — sole authentication factor in CA-OS |
| PAN | Permanent Account Number — 10-character income tax identifier |
| POSH | Prevention of Sexual Harassment Act 2013 — Indian workplace harassment law |
| QRMP | Quarterly Return Monthly Payment — GST scheme for taxpayers with turnover < ₹5 crore |
| RLS | Row-Level Security — PostgreSQL feature used for multi-tenant data isolation |
| SAC | Services Accounting Code — GST classification code for services |
| TAN | Tax Deduction Account Number — required for TDS deductors |
| TDS | Tax Deducted at Source — withholding tax on specified payments |
| WABA | WhatsApp Business Account — Meta's business-tier WhatsApp access |
| WhatsApp Cloud API | Meta's direct WhatsApp Business API (versus managed providers like WATI/Interakt) |

# 31. WHATSAPP BOT LOGIC — COMPLETE STATE MACHINE

## 31.1 Conversation States

Every WhatsApp conversation exists in one of four states:

| State | Description | Who Handles |
|---|---|---|
| `bot_idle` | No active conversation; bot will respond to new messages | AI Bot |
| `bot_active` | Bot is in an active flow (document request, Q&A) | AI Bot |
| `human_requested` | Client requested human; waiting for CA to take over | Queued for CA |
| `human_active` | CA has taken over; bot is silent | CA/Staff |

## 31.2 Inbound Message Handling by State

**State: `bot_idle`**  
New message arrives → classify intent → route:
- First message from client ever → send greeting + menu
- Document-related → acknowledge, log, create document record
- Question → classify type → retrieve context → generate AI draft → notify CA
- Government notice attached → high-priority task creation → acknowledgment to client
- Complaint signal detected → immediate CA alert + human takeover initiated

**State: `bot_active` (within a document collection flow)**  
Bot expects a specific document. If document received → verify type matches request → acknowledge → update task → check if all documents now complete.  
If unrelated message received → acknowledge + re-prompt for the expected document.  
If 3 days pass with no response → escalate to CA.

**State: `human_requested` or `human_active`**  
Bot is completely silent. All incoming messages logged but no automated responses sent. CA sees all messages in WhatsApp Console. CA can switch conversation back to bot mode from the console.

## 31.3 Document Collection Flow

```
Bot → Client: "Hello Ravi, for filing your GSTR-3B for October,
               we need the following documents:
               1. Bank Statement (October)
               2. Purchase Register (October)
               3. Sales Invoice Summary

               Please reply with the document or tap the document icon to upload."

Client → Bot: [Uploads PDF]

Bot (internal): Classify uploaded document type
               If matches expected → acknowledge + log
               If doesn't match → "Thank you! This appears to be [detected_type].
                                   We still need your Bank Statement for October."

Bot → Client: "Thank you! We've received your Bank Statement. ✅
               Still awaiting:
               📋 Purchase Register
               📋 Sales Invoice Summary"

[After all docs received]
Bot → Client: "All documents received! ✅
               We'll complete your GSTR-3B filing by 18 November.
               We'll notify you once filed."
```

## 31.4 Template vs. AI Response Decision

| Scenario | Response Type |
|---|---|
| Document request (scheduled reminder) | Pre-approved Meta template |
| Document receipt acknowledgment | Pre-approved Meta template |
| Filing completion notification | Pre-approved Meta template |
| Invoice payment request | Pre-approved Meta template |
| Client asks a question | AI-generated (Claude claude-3-5-haiku) → CA review |
| Client reports an issue | AI-generated draft + immediate CA alert |
| Client sends first message ever | Pre-approved welcome template |

**Why templates for routine messages:** Meta requires pre-approved templates for business-initiated conversations. Templates are reliable, fast, and cheaper per message than session messages.

---

# 32. MULTI-TENANT SECURITY MODEL — DEEP DIVE

## 32.1 Layers of Tenant Isolation

CA-OS implements **4 independent layers** of multi-tenant isolation:

**Layer 1: Application Layer (ABAC)**  
Every API endpoint has an ABAC check: user's `firm_id` from JWT must match the `firm_id` of the requested resource. Implemented as FastAPI dependency functions.

**Layer 2: Database Layer (Row-Level Security)**  
PostgreSQL RLS policy on every table:
```sql
CREATE POLICY firm_isolation ON compliance_tasks
  USING (firm_id = current_setting('app.firm_id')::uuid);
```
Application sets `app.firm_id` at start of each transaction:
```python
await conn.execute("SET LOCAL app.firm_id = :firm_id", {"firm_id": str(user.firm_id)})
```

**Layer 3: S3 Object Key Namespacing**  
All S3 object keys prefixed with `{firm_id}/`:
```
caos-documents-prod/{firm_id}/{client_id}/{document_id}/{filename}
```
Pre-signed URLs are generated server-side for specific object keys only — a user from Firm A can never get a pre-signed URL for Firm B's object.

**Layer 4: Celery Task Context**  
Every Celery task carries `firm_id` in its parameters. AI agents operating on behalf of a firm can only query data for that firm. Cross-firm data access in a Celery task is architecturally impossible — the task only ever holds one `firm_id`.

## 32.2 JWT Security Details

**Access token (15-minute expiry):**
```json
{
  "sub": "user_uuid",
  "firm_id": "firm_uuid",
  "role": "firm_owner",
  "plan": "professional",
  "plan_features": ["ai_drafts", "whatsapp_ai", "unlimited_clients"],
  "iat": 1697360000,
  "exp": 1697360900,
  "jti": "unique_token_id"
}
```

**RS256 (asymmetric) signing:**
- Private key: only the API server knows it (in AWS Secrets Manager). Used to sign tokens.
- Public key: distributed to any service that needs to verify tokens (future microservices scenario).
- Algorithm confusion attack prevention: API explicitly rejects tokens with `alg: "HS256"` in the header.

**JWT revocation (for logout and security events):**  
JWTs are stateless — they can't be individually revoked. Mitigation: short 15-minute expiry means compromised access tokens have limited lifetime. Refresh tokens can be revoked immediately (deleted from Redis). "Logout all sessions" deletes all refresh tokens for the user.

## 32.3 Permission Enforcement Code Pattern

```python
# app/core/dependencies.py

async def require_firm_owner(
    current_user: User = Depends(get_current_user)
) -> User:
    if current_user.role != "firm_owner":
        raise HTTPException(403, "Firm owner access required")
    return current_user

async def require_client_access(
    client_id: UUID,
    current_user: User = Depends(get_current_user),
    db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_db)
) -> Client:
    """
    Verifies user can access the specified client.
    - firm_owner: can access any client in their firm
    - ca_staff_senior: can access any client (or only assigned, per firm settings)
    - ca_staff_junior/articleship: only if in assigned_staff_ids
    """
    client = await db.get(Client, client_id)
    if not client or client.firm_id != current_user.firm_id:
        raise HTTPException(404, "Client not found")

    if current_user.role in ("firm_owner", "ca_staff_senior"):
        return client

    if current_user.id not in (client.assigned_staff_ids or []):
        raise HTTPException(403, "Access to this client not permitted")

    return client
```

```
You are a senior Indian Chartered Accountant and tax litigator with 15 years of practice.
Your task is to draft a formal, professional GST demand notice response letter

FIRM CONTEXT:

- Client Name: {client_name}

Format: Formal Indian legal correspondence

Structure: Reference to notice → factual response to each allegation →
           legal basis (cite relevant CGST/IGST Act sections if certain) →
           request for relief or stay
Language: Formal, respectful, precise — standard CA-client representation style

CONSTRAINTS:
- Do NOT invent facts
- Do NOT cite case law you are not certain exists — use "relevant judicial precedents" instead
- Do NOT make admissions of liability
- Do NOT use informal language

{notice_text}

{ca_notes}

{grounds_of_dispute}

```

# 34. REAL-TIME ARCHITECTURE — WEBSOCKET PROTOCOL

## 34.1 Connection Lifecycle

**Connection establishment:**
```
Client → Server: GET /ws/{firmId}?token={access_token}
Server: Validate JWT → validate firm_id matches JWT firm_id → accept connection
Server → Client: {"type": "connected", "firm_id": "uuid", "user_id": "uuid", "server_id": "i-1234"}
```

**Heartbeat (every 30 seconds):**
```
Server → Client: {"type": "ping"}
Client → Server: {"type": "pong"}
```
If no pong received within 10 seconds: server closes connection. Client reconnects with exponential backoff (2s, 4s, 8s, 16s, max 60s).

**Graceful disconnect:**
```
Client → Server: {"type": "disconnect"}
Server: Remove from firm channel, close connection cleanly
```

## 34.2 Event Types

**Server → Client events:**

| Event Type | Payload | Consumer |
|---|---|---|
| `task.status_changed` | `{task_id, client_id, old_status, new_status, updated_by}` | Dashboard, Kanban, Compliance Calendar |
| `document.received` | `{document_id, client_id, client_name, document_category}` | Dashboard pending count, Documents page |
| `whatsapp.message_received` | `{conversation_id, client_id, client_name, message_preview, unread_count}` | WhatsApp Console |
| `ai.draft_ready` | `{draft_id, draft_type, client_name, question_preview}` | AI Drafts page, notification bell |
| `invoice.paid` | `{invoice_id, client_id, client_name, amount}` | Billing page, dashboard |
| `task.assigned` | `{task_id, client_name, task_type, due_date}` | Staff's task view |
| `system.announcement` | `{message, severity, action_url?}` | Global notification banner |

## 34.3 Redis Pub/Sub for Multi-Server Broadcasting

When Server A processes a task status change and wants to notify all connected clients of Firm X (who may be connected to Server A, B, or C):

```python
# In compliance_service.py (runs on Server A)
async def update_task_status(task_id, new_status, firm_id):
    # Update database
    await db.execute(...)
    
    # Publish to Redis channel for this firm
    event = {
        "type": "task.status_changed",
        "firm_id": str(firm_id),
        "payload": {
            "task_id": str(task_id),
            "new_status": new_status,
            ...
        }
    }
    await redis.publish(f"firm:{firm_id}", json.dumps(event))

# In WebSocket handler (runs on all servers)
async def redis_subscriber():
    pubsub = redis.pubsub()
    await pubsub.psubscribe("firm:*")  # Subscribe to all firm channels
    async for message in pubsub.listen():
        event = json.loads(message["data"])
        firm_id = event["firm_id"]
        # Find all WebSocket connections for this firm on THIS server
        connections = active_connections.get(firm_id, [])
        for ws in connections:
            await ws.send_json(event["payload"])
```

# 36. DATABASE QUERY OPTIMIZATION PLAYBOOK

## 36.1 Most Critical Queries and Their Optimization

### Query 1: Dashboard — Today's Deadlines Count
**Frequency:** Every 5 minutes per firm (cached); 30 seconds per dashboard load (served from cache)

```sql
-- Optimized query (runs against cache miss only)
SELECT COUNT(*) as overdue_count,
       COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE due_date = CURRENT_DATE) as today_count,
       COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE due_date BETWEEN CURRENT_DATE + 1 AND CURRENT_DATE + 7) as week_count
FROM compliance_tasks
WHERE firm_id = :firm_id
  AND status NOT IN ('completed', 'filed', 'waived')
  AND deleted_at IS NULL;
```

**Covering index:**
```sql
CREATE INDEX idx_tasks_dashboard
ON compliance_tasks (firm_id, status, due_date)
WHERE status NOT IN ('completed', 'filed', 'waived') AND deleted_at IS NULL;
```

### Query 2: Client List with Compliance Metrics
**Frequency:** On client list page load; most expensive query in the system

```sql
SELECT
    c.id, c.name, c.gstin, c.client_type,
    c.compliance_health_score, c.churn_risk,
    c.compliance_services, c.assigned_primary_staff_id,
    COUNT(t.id) FILTER (WHERE t.status = 'overdue') as overdue_count,
    COUNT(d.id) FILTER (WHERE d.ocr_status = 'pending' AND d.virus_scan_status = 'clean') as pending_doc_count,
    COALESCE(SUM(i.total_amount) FILTER (WHERE i.status = 'overdue'), 0) as outstanding_amount
FROM clients c
LEFT JOIN compliance_tasks t ON t.client_id = c.id AND t.deleted_at IS NULL
LEFT JOIN documents d ON d.client_id = c.id
LEFT JOIN invoices i ON i.client_id = c.id
WHERE c.firm_id = :firm_id
  AND c.archived_at IS NULL
GROUP BY c.id
ORDER BY c.compliance_health_score ASC  -- Most at-risk first as default
LIMIT 25 OFFSET :offset;
```

**Optimization strategy:** This query is expensive. Materialize the aggregates:
- `overdue_count` stored directly on `clients` table (updated via trigger on task status change)
- `pending_documents_count` stored on `clients` table (updated via trigger on document record changes)
- `outstanding_amount` stored on `clients` table (updated via trigger on invoice status changes)

The client list query then becomes a simple indexed scan — no joins needed for the list view.

### Query 3: Compliance Calendar Month View
**Frequency:** High during month boundaries; moderate otherwise

```sql
SELECT
    ct.id, ct.task_type, ct.due_date, ct.status, ct.priority,
    ct.documents_ready, ct.assigned_to_id,
    c.name as client_name, c.gstin,
    u.full_name as assigned_to_name
FROM compliance_tasks ct
JOIN clients c ON c.id = ct.client_id
LEFT JOIN users u ON u.id = ct.assigned_to_id
WHERE ct.firm_id = :firm_id
  AND ct.due_date >= :month_start
  AND ct.due_date <= :month_end
  AND ct.status != 'waived'
ORDER BY ct.due_date, ct.priority DESC;
```

**Index:**
```sql
CREATE INDEX idx_tasks_calendar
ON compliance_tasks (firm_id, due_date, status)
INCLUDE (task_type, priority, documents_ready, assigned_to_id, client_id);
```

## 36.2 Query Anti-Patterns to Avoid

| Anti-Pattern | Problem | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| `SELECT *` | Fetches unnecessary columns, prevents index-only scans | Always specify required columns |
| `WHERE status NOT IN (...)` with large NOT IN list | Cannot use index efficiently | Use `status IN (...)` for the positive set |
| Joining then filtering | Joins many rows before filtering | Filter before joining with subqueries or CTEs |
| `LIKE '%term%'` | Cannot use B-tree index | Use `tsvector` full-text search |
| Missing index on FK columns | Full table scans on JOINs | Index every FK column |
| N+1 in Python code | 1 query for list + 1 per item | `selectinload()` or `joinedload()` in SQLAlchemy |

---

---

# 37. FRONTEND STATE MANAGEMENT ARCHITECTURE

## 37.1 State Management Strategy

**Tool:** Zustand (lightweight, atomic, no boilerplate)

**Rationale over Redux:** CA-OS has moderate frontend complexity. Redux overhead (actions, reducers, middleware) is unjustified. Zustand provides simple atomic state with excellent TypeScript support and minimal boilerplate.

**State categories:**

| Store | Contents | Persistence |
|---|---|---|
| `authStore` | Current user, JWT, firm, plan, features | localStorage (for hydration) |
| `firmStore` | Firm settings, team members, subscription | Session only |
| `notificationStore` | Unread notifications, notification drawer state | Session only |
| `uiStore` | Sidebar collapsed state, active modals, loading states | Session only |
| `complianceStore` | Active filters, calendar view mode, selected month | URL params (for shareability) |

## 37.2 Server State Management

**Tool:** TanStack Query (React Query v5)

Used for all API data that needs caching, background refetching, and cache invalidation:

```typescript
// Compliance tasks — auto-refetch when window regains focus
const { data: tasks, isLoading } = useQuery({
  queryKey: ['compliance-tasks', firmId, { month, year, status }],
  queryFn: () => api.compliance.getTasks({ month, year, status }),
  staleTime: 60_000,           // Data fresh for 60 seconds
  gcTime: 300_000,             // Keep in cache for 5 minutes
  refetchOnWindowFocus: true,
  refetchInterval: 60_000,     // Background refresh every 60 seconds
})

// Invalidate on task update
const mutation = useMutation({
  mutationFn: api.compliance.updateTask,
  onSuccess: () => {
    queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['compliance-tasks'] })
    queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['dashboard-metrics'] })
  }
})
```

## 37.3 Real-Time Updates via WebSocket

```typescript
// useWebSocket.ts — subscribes to firm's real-time events
function useWebSocket(firmId: string) {
  useEffect(() => {
    const ws = new WebSocket(`wss://api.caos.in/ws/${firmId}?token=${getAccessToken()}`)

    ws.onmessage = (event) => {
      const message = JSON.parse(event.data)
      
      switch (message.type) {
        case 'task.status_changed':
          queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['compliance-tasks'] })
          queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['dashboard-metrics'] })
          break
        case 'document.received':
          queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['documents'] })
          notificationStore.addNotification(message.payload)
          break
        case 'ai.draft_ready':
          notificationStore.addNotification(message.payload)
          break
        case 'invoice.paid':
          queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['invoices'] })
          break
      }
    }

    // Heartbeat
    const heartbeat = setInterval(() => {
      if (ws.readyState === WebSocket.OPEN) {
        ws.send(JSON.stringify({ type: 'pong' }))
      }
    }, 30_000)

    return () => {
      clearInterval(heartbeat)
      ws.close()
    }
  }, [firmId])
}
```

# 38. ERROR HANDLING FRAMEWORK

## 38.1 Backend Error Hierarchy

```python
# app/core/exceptions.py

class CAOSError(Exception):
    """Base exception for all CA-OS errors."""
    status_code: int = 500
    error_code: str = "internal_error"
    message: str = "An unexpected error occurred."

class NotFoundError(CAOSError):
    status_code = 404
    error_code = "not_found"

class ForbiddenError(CAOSError):
    status_code = 403
    error_code = "forbidden"

class ValidationError(CAOSError):
    status_code = 422
    error_code = "validation_error"

class RateLimitError(CAOSError):
    status_code = 429
    error_code = "rate_limited"

class PlanLimitError(CAOSError):
    status_code = 402
    error_code = "plan_limit_exceeded"
    message = "This feature requires a higher plan."

class ExternalServiceError(CAOSError):
    status_code = 502
    error_code = "external_service_error"

class AIServiceError(ExternalServiceError):
    error_code = "ai_service_error"

class WhatsAppServiceError(ExternalServiceError):
    error_code = "whatsapp_service_error"
```

## 38.2 Global Error Handler

```python
# main.py

@app.exception_handler(CAOSError)
async def caos_error_handler(request: Request, exc: CAOSError):
    return JSONResponse(
        status_code=exc.status_code,
        content={
            "error": exc.error_code,
            "message": exc.message,
            "request_id": request.headers.get("X-Request-ID", "")
        }
    )

@app.exception_handler(Exception)
async def generic_error_handler(request: Request, exc: Exception):
    # Log to Sentry and application logs — but never expose details to client
    logger.error("Unhandled exception", exc_info=exc, extra={"request_id": request.headers.get("X-Request-ID")})
    sentry_sdk.capture_exception(exc)
    return JSONResponse(
        status_code=500,
        content={
            "error": "internal_error",
            "message": "An unexpected error occurred. Our team has been notified.",
            "request_id": request.headers.get("X-Request-ID", "")
        }
    )
```

## 38.3 Frontend Error Handling

**API call error handling (via TanStack Query):**
```typescript
// Global error handler in QueryClient config
const queryClient = new QueryClient({
  defaultOptions: {
    queries: {
      retry: (failureCount, error) => {
        // Don't retry 4xx errors (client errors)
        if (error.status >= 400 && error.status < 500) return false
        // Retry network errors and 5xx up to 3 times
        return failureCount < 3
      },
      retryDelay: (attempt) => Math.min(1000 * 2 ** attempt, 30000)
    },
    mutations: {
      onError: (error) => {
        if (error.status === 429) {
          toast.error("Too many requests. Please wait a moment and try again.")
        } else if (error.status === 402) {
          toast.error("Feature not available on your current plan.", {
            action: { label: "Upgrade", onClick: () => router.push('/settings/billing') }
          })
        } else if (error.status >= 500) {
          toast.error("Something went wrong. Our team has been notified.")
        }
      }
    }
  }
})
```

# 39. LOCALIZATION & INTERNATIONALIZATION

## 39.1 v1.0 Scope — English Only

CA-OS v1.0 is English-only. However, the codebase is architected for localization from day one — no hardcoded user-facing strings.

**Frontend:** All user-facing strings in `lib/constants/strings.ts`. Component text always from string constants, not hardcoded.

**Backend:** All notification template content and error messages stored in the database with a `language` column (`en` by default).

## 39.2 Phase 2 — Hindi and Telugu

**Target:** Hindi (hi) for North India CA firms; Telugu (te) for Hyderabad/Telangana.

**Frontend localization:** `next-intl` library for Next.js App Router.  
**WhatsApp templates:** All Meta-approved templates to be submitted in Hindi and Telugu variants.  
**AI prompts:** Hindi and Telugu system prompt variants for client-facing AI responses.  
**Client portal:** Full translation — client dashboard, document upload instructions, invoice descriptions.

## 39.3 Currency and Number Formatting

**Always used in CA-OS:**
- Currency symbol: `₹` (not "INR" in UI)
- Indian number system: 1,00,000 (not 100,000); 10,00,000 (not 1,000,000)
- Amount in words: Indian system ("Ten Lakh Thirty Thousand Only")
- Date format: DD/MM/YYYY in UI, ISO 8601 in API
- Financial year display: "FY 2025-26" (not "FY 2025-2026")

# 40. ACCESSIBILITY IMPLEMENTATION GUIDE

## 40.1 Keyboard Navigation

**Tab order:** Logical tab order following visual layout. Custom tab order overridden via `tabIndex` only when absolutely necessary.

**Focus management:**
- Modal opens: focus moves to first interactive element inside modal
- Modal closes: focus returns to the element that triggered the modal
- Page navigation: focus moves to the page `<h1>` on route change
- Toast notifications: announced via `aria-live="polite"` but do not steal focus

**Keyboard shortcuts (global):**

| Shortcut | Action |
|---|---|
| `Cmd/Ctrl + K` | Open command palette |
| `Cmd/Ctrl + N` | Quick add client |
| `Escape` | Close modal/drawer/dropdown |
| `?` | Open keyboard shortcuts help |

## 40.2 Screen Reader Support

**Page titles:** Every page has a unique, descriptive `<title>` tag: `"Dashboard — RK Associates — CA-OS"`.

**Headings:** Logical heading hierarchy (`<h1>` → `<h2>` → `<h3>`) on every page. No heading levels skipped.

**Landmarks:** `<main>`, `<nav>`, `<aside>`, `<header>`, `<footer>` used correctly.

**Data tables (Compliance Calendar, Client List, Invoices):**
```html
<table>
  <caption>October 2025 Compliance Tasks</caption>
  <thead>
    <tr>
      <th scope="col">Client</th>
      <th scope="col">Task Type</th>
      <th scope="col">Due Date</th>
      <th scope="col">Status</th>
      <th scope="col">Actions</th>
    </tr>
  </thead>
  ...
</table>
```

**Status indicators (color + icon, never color alone):**
```html
<!-- Good: color + icon + text -->
<span class="status-overdue">
  <AlertCircle aria-hidden="true" />
  <span>Overdue</span>
</span>

<!-- Bad: color alone -->
<span class="status-overdue" aria-label="overdue"></span>
```

## 40.3 WCAG 2.1 Level AA Compliance Checklist

- [ ] 1.1.1 Non-text Content: All images have alt text; decorative images have `alt=""`
- [ ] 1.3.1 Info and Relationships: Table headers, form labels, ARIA roles correct
- [ ] 1.4.1 Use of Color: Status never conveyed by color alone (always + icon or text)
- [ ] 1.4.3 Contrast: All text ≥ 4.5:1 contrast ratio (verified with axe DevTools)
- [ ] 1.4.4 Resize Text: Page functional up to 200% zoom without horizontal scroll
- [ ] 2.1.1 Keyboard: All functionality accessible via keyboard
- [ ] 2.4.1 Bypass Blocks: Skip to main content link on every page
- [ ] 2.4.3 Focus Order: Logical focus order throughout
- [ ] 2.4.7 Focus Visible: Focus indicator visible and high-contrast
- [ ] 3.2.3 Consistent Navigation: Navigation in same location across all pages
- [ ] 3.3.1 Error Identification: Form errors identified in text
- [ ] 3.3.2 Labels or Instructions: All form inputs have visible labels
- [ ] 4.1.2 Name, Role, Value: All UI components have accessible name, role, state

# 41. MOBILE RESPONSIVENESS SPECIFICATION

## 41.1 Breakpoint Behavior

### Mobile (< 768px)

**Navigation:** Sidebar hidden by default; hamburger button in top nav opens sidebar as an off-canvas drawer with overlay.

**Dashboard:** 4 metric cards stack as 2×2 grid. All row panels become single-column stacked. Quick action bar becomes a floating action button (FAB) in bottom-right corner.

**Client List:** Table collapses to card-based list. Each client shown as a card: name prominent, GSTIN small below, health score dial, quick actions (tap to expand).

**Compliance Calendar:** Calendar view hidden on mobile (too small to be useful). List view is default. Board view available via toggle.

**WhatsApp Console:** Full-screen conversation list. Tapping a conversation opens full-screen message thread. Back button returns to list.

**Forms (Add Client, Create Invoice):** Single-column layout. All inputs full-width. Submit button full-width at bottom.

**Touch targets:** All interactive elements minimum 44×44px (Apple HIG / Material Design recommendation).

### Tablet (768px – 1023px)

**Sidebar:** Shown but auto-collapses to icon-only mode (56px wide). Tap icons to navigate; sidebar label visible on hover (desktop) or on tap (tablet).

**Dashboard:** 4 metric cards in a 2×2 grid. Second-row panels side-by-side. Third-row panels become 2+1 (Staff Workload + AI Activity side-by-side; Billing below).

**Client List:** Full table but with fewer columns visible; horizontal scroll for additional columns.

**Compliance Calendar:** All views available. Calendar view works well on tablet landscape.

## 41.2 Touch Gesture Support

**Swipe left on a client list row:** Reveals quick action buttons (Send Reminder, Archive).

**Pull-to-refresh on mobile:** Dashboard, client list, and compliance calendar support pull-to-refresh gesture (triggers data refetch).

**Pinch-to-zoom:** Disabled on the app (interferes with table interactions). Users zoom via browser controls if needed.

# 42. SEARCH ARCHITECTURE — DEEP DIVE

## 42.1 PostgreSQL Full-Text Search Implementation

**Setup for clients table:**
```sql
-- Add search vector column
ALTER TABLE clients ADD COLUMN search_vector tsvector;

-- Create GIN index
CREATE INDEX idx_clients_search ON clients USING GIN(search_vector);

-- Create trigger function
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION update_client_search_vector()
RETURNS TRIGGER AS $$
BEGIN
  NEW.search_vector :=
    setweight(to_tsvector('english', COALESCE(NEW.name, '')), 'A') ||
    setweight(to_tsvector('simple', COALESCE(NEW.gstin, '')), 'B') ||
    setweight(to_tsvector('simple', COALESCE(NEW.pan, '')), 'B') ||
    setweight(to_tsvector('english', COALESCE(NEW.primary_contact_name, '')), 'C');
  RETURN NEW;
END;
$$ LANGUAGE plpgsql;

-- Attach trigger
CREATE TRIGGER clients_search_update
  BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF name, gstin, pan, primary_contact_name
  ON clients
  FOR EACH ROW
  EXECUTE FUNCTION update_client_search_vector();
```

**Why `to_tsvector('simple', ...)` for GSTIN and PAN?**  
The `'simple'` dictionary does not apply stemming or stopword removal — important for alphanumeric identifiers where every character matters. `'english'` is used for name fields where stemming (e.g., "consulting" matches "consult") is helpful.

**Search query:**
```python
# In clients service
from sqlalchemy import func

query = select(Client).where(
    Client.firm_id == firm_id,
    Client.archived_at.is_(None),
    Client.search_vector.op('@@')(
        func.websearch_to_tsquery('english', search_term)
    )
).order_by(
    func.ts_rank(
        Client.search_vector,
        func.websearch_to_tsquery('english', search_term)
    ).desc()
)
```

`websearch_to_tsquery` is used over `plainto_tsquery` because it supports quoted phrases (`"techcorp private"`) and exclusions (`-ltd`), matching user expectations from Google-like search behavior.

## 42.2 Command Palette Search

The global command palette (`Cmd+K`) searches across multiple entity types simultaneously:

```typescript
// lib/api/search.ts
async function globalSearch(query: string) {
  const [clients, tasks, documents] = await Promise.all([
    api.clients.search(query, { limit: 5 }),
    api.compliance.searchTasks(query, { limit: 5 }),
    api.documents.search(query, { limit: 3 })
  ])
  
  return {
    clients: clients.data,
    tasks: tasks.data,
    documents: documents.data,
    quick_actions: getRelevantQuickActions(query)  // Pattern-matched suggestions
  }
}
```

Results are debounced (200ms) and streamed progressively as each parallel request completes (not waiting for all three).

# 43. AUDIT & COMPLIANCE REPORTING

## 43.1 Audit Reports Available to Firm Owner

**Report 1: Client Compliance Summary Report**  
Per client, per financial year: all compliance tasks, filing dates, ARNs, penalties (if any). Exportable as PDF for client records.

**Report 2: Staff Activity Report**  
Per staff member: tasks completed, time logged, billing generated. Helps with staff performance review.

**Report 3: Billing Summary Report**  
Total billing per client per month/quarter/year. Revenue breakdown by service type. Outstanding payments.

**Report 4: Document Audit Trail**  
Per client: all documents received (date, source, type) and all documents sent (filed returns, certificates).

**Report 5: AI Usage Report**  
Total AI drafts generated, by type, by staff member. Quality ratings average. Billing implications.

## 43.2 Audit Reports Available to Super Admin (Platform Level)

**Report 6: Firm Activity Audit**  
All user actions within a firm for a specified date range. Exportable for legal/regulatory requests.

**Report 7: Data Access Log**  
All data access events by Support Agents or Super Admins to firm data. Includes justification documented at time of access.

**Report 8: Security Events Report**  
All security-relevant events: failed login attempts, MFA bypasses, unusual IP logins, large data exports.

## 43.3 Regulatory Export Format

When a firm owner requests a data export (DPDPA compliance, "right to access"):
1. All client records (CSV)
2. All compliance task records (CSV)
3. All document metadata (CSV — not the files themselves; download links provided separately)
4. All invoice records (CSV)
5. All audit log entries for this firm (CSV)
6. Summary of AI drafts generated (CSV)

Packaged as a ZIP file, accessible via a time-limited download link (24-hour expiry), sent to the firm owner's registered email.

# 44. SUBSCRIPTION & BILLING LOGIC

## 44.1 Plan Feature Gates

Feature availability is checked at two levels:

**Level 1: JWT (Fast check)**  
`plan_features` array in the JWT contains the features the firm's plan allows. Frontend and backend both check this before allowing access.

**Level 2: Database (Authoritative check)**  
For features with usage limits (AI drafts, WhatsApp messages), the actual usage counter in Redis (incremented atomically) is checked against the plan limit stored in the `firms` table.

## 44.2 Plan Limits Enforcement

**AI Draft Limits:**
```python
async def check_ai_draft_limit(firm_id: UUID, db: AsyncSession) -> None:
    """
    Atomically check and increment AI draft usage counter.
    Raises PlanLimitError if limit exceeded.
    """
    firm = await db.get(Firm, firm_id)
    
    # Check Redis counter
    current_usage = await redis.get(f"ai_drafts_used:{firm_id}:{current_month()}")
    current_usage = int(current_usage or 0)
    
    if current_usage >= firm.monthly_ai_draft_limit:
        raise PlanLimitError(
            message=f"Monthly AI draft limit of {firm.monthly_ai_draft_limit} reached. "
                   f"Upgrade your plan for more drafts."
        )
    
    # Increment usage counter (atomic)
    await redis.incr(f"ai_drafts_used:{firm_id}:{current_month()}")
    
    # Set TTL to expire at end of month (if not already set)
    await redis.expire(
        f"ai_drafts_used:{firm_id}:{current_month()}",
        seconds_until_end_of_month()
    )
```

## 44.3 Trial-to-Paid Conversion

**Trial mechanics:**
- 14-day trial of Professional plan for all new signups
- Full access to all Professional features during trial
- No credit card required to start trial

**Trial expiry flow:**
1. T-7 days: Email "Your trial ends in 7 days"
2. T-3 days: Email + in-app banner "Your trial ends in 3 days"
3. T-1 day: WhatsApp + email + prominent in-app banner "Trial expires tomorrow"
4. T-0: Plan downgrades to Starter. Access to Professional features restricted. Data preserved.
5. T+0 after downgrade: In-app upgrade prompt shown prominently on every page
6. T+30: If no payment, account suspended (read-only mode). Data preserved for 90 days.
7. T+120: Account and data scheduled for deletion. Email warning sent.

## 44.4 Razorpay Subscription Webhook Handling

```python
@router.post("/webhooks/razorpay")
async def razorpay_webhook(request: Request, db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_db)):
    # Verify Razorpay signature
    signature = request.headers.get("X-Razorpay-Signature")
    payload = await request.body()
    verify_razorpay_signature(payload, signature, settings.razorpay_webhook_secret)
    
    event = await request.json()
    event_type = event.get("event")
    
    if event_type == "subscription.charged":
        # Payment successful — extend plan_status to 'active'
        subscription_id = event["payload"]["subscription"]["entity"]["id"]
        firm = await get_firm_by_subscription_id(subscription_id, db)
        firm.plan_status = "active"
        firm.plan_started_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
        await db.commit()
        # Send confirmation email/WhatsApp to firm owner
        
    elif event_type == "subscription.halted":
        # Payment failed — move to suspended with grace period
        firm.plan_status = "payment_failed"
        await db.commit()
        # Alert firm owner with payment retry instructions
        
    elif event_type == "subscription.cancelled":
        firm.plan_status = "cancelled"
        await db.commit()
        # Trigger offboarding flow
    
    return {"status": "processed"}
```

# 46. STAFF WORKLOAD INTELLIGENCE ENGINE

## 46.1 Workload Capacity Model

CA-OS calculates a "capacity score" for each staff member to determine if they are overloaded.

**Capacity calculation:**
```python
def calculate_staff_capacity(
    staff_id: UUID,
    active_tasks: List[ComplianceTask],
    time_period_days: int = 7
) -> Dict:
    """
    Returns capacity metrics for a staff member.
    """
    
    # Weight tasks by urgency
    weighted_task_count = sum(
        TASK_WEIGHTS[task.priority] * urgency_multiplier(task.due_date)
        for task in active_tasks
        if task.assigned_to_id == staff_id
    )
    
    # Capacity thresholds (configurable per firm)
    CAPACITY_GREEN = 40   # <= 40 weighted units: healthy
    CAPACITY_AMBER = 70   # 41–70: near capacity, watch
    CAPACITY_RED = 71     # > 70: overloaded
    
    if weighted_task_count <= CAPACITY_GREEN:
        status = "healthy"
        color = "green"
    elif weighted_task_count <= CAPACITY_AMBER:
        status = "near_capacity"
        color = "amber"
    else:
        status = "overloaded"
        color = "red"
    
    return {
        "staff_id": str(staff_id),
        "weighted_task_count": weighted_task_count,
        "raw_task_count": len([t for t in active_tasks if t.assigned_to_id == staff_id]),
        "capacity_status": status,
        "capacity_color": color,
        "overdue_task_count": len([t for t in active_tasks if t.assigned_to_id == staff_id and t.status == "overdue"]),
        "tasks_due_this_week": len([t for t in active_tasks if t.assigned_to_id == staff_id and t.due_date <= date.today() + timedelta(days=7)])
    }

# Priority weights
TASK_WEIGHTS = {
    "critical": 5,
    "high": 3,
    "medium": 2,
    "low": 1
}

def urgency_multiplier(due_date: date) -> float:
    days_until_due = (due_date - date.today()).days
    if days_until_due < 0:    return 3.0  # Overdue
    if days_until_due <= 2:   return 2.0  # Due very soon
    if days_until_due <= 7:   return 1.5  # Due this week
    return 1.0                             # Due later
```

## 46.2 Smart Task Assignment Suggestions

When a Firm Owner assigns a new task without specifying a staff member, CA-OS suggests the best available staff:

```python
def suggest_task_assignee(
    firm_id: UUID,
    task_type: str,
    client_id: UUID,
    staff_capacity_data: List[Dict]
) -> Optional[UUID]:
    """
    Suggests the best staff member for a new task based on:
    1. Current capacity (prefer least loaded)
    2. Historical assignment (prefer staff who handles this client regularly)
    3. Specialization (prefer staff with this task type experience)
    """
    # Filter out overloaded staff
    available = [s for s in staff_capacity_data if s["capacity_status"] != "overloaded"]
    
    if not available:
        return None  # No suitable staff — return None, let CA decide
    
    # Score each available staff member
    scores = {}
    for staff in available:
        score = 0
        # Lower workload = higher score
        score += (100 - staff["weighted_task_count"])
        # Regular handler of this client = bonus
        if staff["frequently_handles_client_ids"] and client_id in staff["frequently_handles_client_ids"]:
            score += 30
        # Experienced with this task type = bonus
        if staff["frequently_completed_task_types"] and task_type in staff["frequently_completed_task_types"]:
            score += 20
        scores[staff["staff_id"]] = score
    
    # Return highest-scoring staff member
    return max(scores, key=scores.get)
```

# 47. DOCUMENT INTELLIGENCE PIPELINE

## 47.1 Complete Document Processing Pipeline

```
Client uploads document
        ↓
Frontend requests pre-signed S3 URL from API
        ↓
File uploads directly to S3
        ↓
Frontend confirms upload to API
        ↓
API creates document record (status: virus_scan=pending, ocr=pending)
        ↓
S3 ObjectCreated event → Lambda triggered
        ↓
Lambda: Download file + run ClamAV
        ↓
    [INFECTED]                    [CLEAN]
        ↓                             ↓
Move to quarantine bucket    Update document.virus_scan_status = 'clean'
Alert CA firm                         ↓
                             Celery task: process_ocr_extraction
                                         ↓
                             Determine processing strategy:
                             - Text-based PDF → pdf.js extraction (free)
                             - Scanned/image PDF → Google Document AI
                             - Image file → Google Document AI
                                         ↓
                             Extract structured data
                                         ↓
                             Post-process: normalize amounts, dates, GST numbers
                                         ↓
                             Generate AI summary via Claude claude-3-5-haiku
                                         ↓
                             Update document record:
                             - extracted_data_json = {...}
                             - ai_summary = "..."
                             - ocr_status = 'completed'
                                         ↓
                             Check if this completes a document request
                                         ↓
                             If yes: update compliance_task.documents_ready = true
                                         ↓
                             WebSocket event to CA dashboard
                                         ↓
                             Create notification for CA
```

## 47.2 Extraction Data Schemas by Document Type

**Bank Statement extraction output:**
```json
{
  "document_type": "bank_statement",
  "account_name": "TECHCORP PRIVATE LIMITED",
  "account_number": "1234567890",
  "bank_name": "HDFC Bank",
  "ifsc": "HDFC0001234",
  "period": {"month": 10, "year": 2025},
  "opening_balance": 125000.00,
  "closing_balance": 287500.00,
  "total_credits": 450000.00,
  "total_debits": 287500.00,
  "transaction_count": 47,
  "confidence_score": 0.92
}
```

**GST Invoice extraction output:**
```json
{
  "document_type": "tax_invoice",
  "invoice_number": "INV-2025-001",

```

# 48. ANALYTICS DATA MODEL

## 48.1 Analytics Tables

### `firm_daily_metrics`
Pre-aggregated daily statistics per firm for fast analytics dashboard loading.

| Column | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| id | UUID PK | |
| firm_id | UUID FK | |
| date | DATE NOT NULL | |
| active_clients | INTEGER | Count of non-archived clients |
| compliance_tasks_completed | INTEGER | Tasks completed on this date |
| compliance_tasks_overdue | INTEGER | Snapshot of overdue at end of day |
| documents_received | INTEGER | Documents uploaded on this date |
| ai_drafts_generated | INTEGER | |
| ai_drafts_used | INTEGER | Drafts actually sent to clients |
| whatsapp_messages_sent | INTEGER | |
| whatsapp_messages_received | INTEGER | |
| invoices_created | INTEGER | |
| invoices_amount | NUMERIC(14,2) | Total amount invoiced |
| payments_received | INTEGER | |
| payments_amount | NUMERIC(14,2) | Total amount received |

**Populated by:** `aggregate_daily_analytics` Celery Beat task (daily midnight IST).  
**Indexed:** `(firm_id, date)` for fast date range queries.

### `client_health_history`
Monthly snapshot of each client's health score for trend analysis.

| Column | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| id | UUID PK | |
| firm_id | UUID FK | |
| client_id | UUID FK | |
| snapshot_month | DATE NOT NULL | First day of month |
| compliance_health_score | SMALLINT | 1–10 |
| penalty_risk_score | SMALLINT | 1–10 |
| churn_risk | ENUM | low/medium/high |
| tasks_completed | INTEGER | Tasks completed that month |
| tasks_overdue | INTEGER | Tasks overdue that month |
| documents_submission_rate | NUMERIC(4,2) | % documents submitted on time |

# 49. API RATE LIMITING RULES — COMPLETE TABLE

## 49.1 All Rate Limits

| Endpoint Pattern | Limit | Window | Per | Response |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `POST /auth/request-otp` | 2 | 5 min | Mobile number | 429 + retry_after |
| `POST /auth/request-otp` | 5 | 5 min | IP address | 429 + retry_after |
| `POST /auth/verify-otp` | 5 | 10 min | Mobile number | 429 + lockout_until |
| `POST /auth/resend-otp` | 2 | 5 min | Mobile number | 429 + retry_after |
| `POST /clients` | 30 | 60 min | Firm | 429 |
| `POST /clients/import-csv` | 2 | 60 min | Firm | 429 |
| `GET /clients` | 100 | 60 min | User | 429 |
| `GET /compliance/calendar` | 60 | 60 min | User | 429 |
| `POST /compliance/reminders/send-bulk` | 2 | 60 min | Firm | 429 |
| `POST /ai/draft` | 10 | 60 min | Firm | 429 |
| `POST /ai/draft` | 50 | 24 hours | Firm (plan-based) | 402 (plan limit) |
| `POST /documents/upload-url` | 20 | 60 min | Firm | 429 |
| `POST /whatsapp/messages/send` | 30 | 60 min | Firm | 429 |
| `POST /billing/invoices` | 20 | 60 min | Firm | 429 |
| All authenticated endpoints | 200 | 60 min | User | 429 |
| All endpoints | 1000 | 60 min | IP (Nginx) | 429 |
| Login page | 10 | 60 min | IP (Nginx) | 429 |

## 49.2 Rate Limit Headers

All 429 responses include:
```
Retry-After: {seconds until reset}
X-RateLimit-Limit: {limit value}
X-RateLimit-Remaining: 0
X-RateLimit-Reset: {Unix timestamp when limit resets}
```

# 50. FINAL ARCHITECTURE DECISION RECORDS (ADRs)

## ADR 001: Modular Monolith over Microservices

**Decision:** Use a modular monolith for v1.0.

**Context:** Small initial team (2–5 engineers), shared transactional data across modules, early-stage product with frequent pivots.

**Rationale:** Microservices overhead (service discovery, distributed tracing, network latency, deployment complexity) exceeds the operational capacity of the initial team. The modular monolith provides clean boundaries that enable future extraction to microservices without rewriting business logic.

**Consequence:** Must maintain strict module boundaries from day one. No direct database queries across module boundaries. Service interfaces only.

**Migration trigger:** Adopt microservices extraction when any single module requires independently scaling (expected at Year 2 for the AI module, which may need GPU-based inference).

---

## ADR 002: PostgreSQL RLS for Multi-Tenancy

**Decision:** Use PostgreSQL Row-Level Security (RLS) as the primary multi-tenancy enforcement mechanism.

**Context:** Need strong tenant isolation; team is experienced with PostgreSQL; Supabase supports RLS natively.

**Alternatives considered:**
- Schema-per-tenant: Rejected — migration complexity with thousands of schemas
- Application-only isolation: Rejected — a single bug could expose all tenant data; defense in depth requires a database-level guard

**Rationale:** RLS provides database-level enforcement independent of application bugs. Combined with application-level ABAC, this creates two independent isolation layers. If the application has a bug and forgets to filter by `firm_id`, the RLS policy catches it.

**Consequence:** All database connections must set `app.firm_id` at the start of each transaction. Slightly more complex connection management but significantly stronger security.

---

## ADR 003: OTP-Only Authentication

**Decision:** No passwords in CA-OS. OTP-based login only.

**Context:** Target users are Indian CA professionals. Indian banking apps use OTP exclusively. Password management (reuse, weak passwords, phishing) is a significant security risk.

**Rationale:** Eliminates the entire class of password-based attacks (credential stuffing, brute force, phishing for passwords). Simpler UX — Indian users are comfortable with OTP. Reduces our storage and management of sensitive credential data.

**Consequence:** Users without a mobile phone cannot log in. Not a real constraint for the target audience. Email-only OTP provided as alternative for users who cannot receive SMS.

---

## ADR 004: Async-First Backend with Celery

**Decision:** FastAPI (async) for all I/O-bound operations; Celery for all time-consuming or async tasks.

**Context:** CA-OS is heavily I/O-bound: LLM API calls (10–30 seconds), WhatsApp API calls, S3 uploads, database queries. A synchronous server would create severe throughput constraints.

**Rationale:** FastAPI's async support allows 1 server instance to handle many concurrent requests efficiently (event loop, not thread pool). Celery offloads tasks that should not block the request-response cycle (OCR, AI drafts, bulk messages).

**Consequence:** Developer discipline required — must use `async def` and `await` correctly throughout; sync libraries must be wrapped in `asyncio.to_thread()` to avoid blocking the event loop.

---

## ADR 005: Client Portal as Separate Auth Namespace

**Decision:** Client portal has a completely separate authentication system from firm-internal users.

**Context:** Clients (business owners) must never be able to access any firm-internal data, even if they somehow obtain a staff member's session.

**Rationale:** A single auth system with separate permissions is theoretically sufficient, but in practice a subtle permission bug could expose firm data to clients. By using a separate JWT secret and separate URL namespace, even a complete bypass of application-level permissions cannot grant a client access to firm-internal APIs.

**Consequence:** Separate OTP flows for firm users and clients. Separate JWT validation middleware. Slightly more code but dramatically stronger security guarantee.

---

## ADR 006: WhatsApp as Primary Communication Channel

**Decision:** WhatsApp is the primary notification and communication channel; email is secondary.

**Context:** Indian business communication culture is WhatsApp-first. Email open rates for Indian SMB owners are < 30%. WhatsApp message open rates > 90%.

**Rationale:** Building CA-OS around WhatsApp aligns with how Indian CA firms actually communicate with clients. Any tool that forces email-first communication will see lower adoption in India.

**Consequence:** Dependency on Meta's WhatsApp API (managed via fallback to SMS). Requirement to comply with Meta's message template approval process. Higher operational complexity but significantly better user engagement.

---

## ADR 007: India-First, Global Second

**Decision:** All data residency, infrastructure, and compliance is India-first. International expansion is Phase 4+.

**Context:** DPDPA 2023 requires data to remain in India for Indian users. AWS Mumbai region supports this.

**Rationale:** Designing for global data residency from day one would add significant complexity (multi-region data sync, GDPR vs DPDPA compliance handling, latency management) without any business benefit at v1.0 scale.

**Consequence:** All AWS resources in ap-south-1. International users (if any) in early phases are served from India (slightly higher latency but acceptable). Architecture reviewed for international expansion in Year 3.

---
